## 12. Scores

| Axis | Score | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| A. Malformed input | **7** | 15 951 structure-aware fuzz executions and an 18 599-offset truncation sweep with ASan+UBSan found no crash, hang or out-of-bounds — but there is no coverage-guided campaign and `fm_png.c`'s inflate was only hand-tested, so a score above 7 would not be defensible |
| B. Correct results | **6** | the JPEG encoder now matches libjpeg at every quality, but two wrong-result defects were confirmed in code no reference tool had touched: the "bilinear" scaler (IMG4-02) and the hardware 4:4:4 path (IMG4-01) |
| C. Errors and resources | **8** | sink/capacity fault injection: every failure returned an error, `out_len` never lied, no leak; nested decode returns `-8`; the one gap is the silently-assumed layout on a `GetInfo` failure (IMG4-S2) |
| D. Memory and timing | **7** | ARM frames measured and consistent with README (2.4 KB worst); 32-bit arithmetic settled by running; encode time measured on the host and estimated for the H7, not measured there |
| E. Documentation | **6** | `hw_jpeg.h:18` promises 4:4:4 that the code does not do (IMG4-01); `include/fm_image.h:254` and README:66 promise bilinear that is nearest (IMG4-02); the rest of the header comments I checked (PROBE budget, BMP variants, codec scratch, `swap16`) are accurate |
| F. Tests | **6** | 11 of 13 plausible mutations caught, but the two encoder edge cases the prompt predicted are exactly the ones that survive, and no test in the suite can reach `top_api/` |
| G. Diagnosability | **6** | the decoders' error codes are distinct and correct (`-12`, `-18`, `-16`, `-15`); but IMG4-01's `FM_ERR_UNSUPPORTED` says nothing about the layout, and `Image_Scale` reports success for a wrong picture (nothing logs) |

**Ready for a product, under these conditions:** the **software** image path (BMP, PNG, JPEG decode and encode) is ready for a product; the **hardware JPEG** path (`hw_jpeg.h`, `hw_jpeg_video.h`) is **not** ready until IMG4-01 is fixed and tried on a board.

**Three things to fix first:**
1. IMG4-01 — the 4:4:4 branch and the `ysz` sizing in `hw_jpeg.h` (and the same `ysz` in `hw_jpeg_video.h`): the default encoder and the default decoder disagree on the commonest layout.
2. IMG4-02 — the bilinear scaler: every photo a slideshow fits to the panel is nearest today.
3. IMG4-S2 — stop guessing 4:2:0 when the header cannot be read; fall back to the software decoder instead.

---

## 13. Where the next round should look

1. **`top_api/simple_image.h` + `board/app_display.h` as a pair, on a stub HAL with a scripted frame buffer.** Both were only read by me; they hold the format, size, effect and rotation state the prompt asks about, and every defect in this part that involved *state across calls* came from this layer. Method: a host harness that drives the state machine through every command and compares the frame buffer with a reference produced by `Image_Scale`/`Image_Convert`.
2. **The hardware-JPEG glue again, with the fix applied and a faithful stub.** IMG4-01 was found by reading the HAL source; the same reading has not been done for `hw_jpeg_video.h`'s MJPEG path, and the HAL's 4:1:1 and CMYK outputs (`JPEG_GetInfo` reports 4:4:4 for both in its `else`) are untested.
3. **`src/fm_png.c` under libFuzzer with a corpus from `test/vectors/`.** Hand-written inflate, no coverage-guided campaign ever, ~1 449 lines. Method: `-fsanitize=fuzzer,address,undefined`, the decoders' entry points, output and work buffers at several sizes.
4. **The JPEG encoder's edge replication and rounding** — the two mutations the suite does not notice (§8). Method: a differential harness that encodes a picture at 4:2:0, decodes with libjpeg, and compares the right and bottom edge columns against libjpeg's own 4:2:0 encoder within 1 level.
5. **`SimpleImage_Resize` / `Rotate` / `Effect` on the frame buffer** with `SIMPLE_IMAGE_SWAP16` 1 and 0. Method: the same harness as (1), checking the byte order at the display boundary against `spi_tft_dma.h`'s expectation.

**Estimate of what is left.** The two S2 findings are both "wrong result returned as success" in paths that no reference tool had been pointed at, and both were found in a few hours of looking — the pattern that produced this project's history of defects. I expect **at least one more S2** in the `SimpleImage`/hardware-glue layer (areas 1 and 2) and **probably none** in the software codecs (PNG/JPEG/BMP), where the differential comparison with Pillow is now broad. An S1 is unlikely: the fuzzing, the truncation sweep and the 32-bit runs found no memory error, and the two confirmed defects are both arithmetic, not memory.

---

## 14. Findings index

```
IMG4-02 | S2 | confirmed | Image | src/fm_image.c:1754-1797 | fix tested yes | test included yes | FM_SCALE_BILINEAR weighs the neighbours 0/1 and reproduces the nearest pixel
IMG4-01 | S2 | confirmed | Image | top_api/hw_jpeg.h:345-362 | fix tested yes | test included yes | hardware JPEG: no 4:4:4 branch, refused or mis-decoded under a header that promises 4:4:4
IMG4-S1 | S3 | suspicion | Image | src/fm_jpeg.c:616-657 | fix tested no | test included no | the JPEG work-buffer claim is held across hw->decode(); nested load gets FM_ERR_BUSY (no deadlock on the host; board unverified)
IMG4-S2 | S2 | suspicion | Image | top_api/hw_jpeg.h:433-440 | fix tested no | test included no | HAL_JPEG_GetInfo failure silently assumes 4:2:0 for any file
```

---

# AUDIT_VIDEO_V4 — external audit V4, Video part

Commit audited: `48a00bc613cac4eb407f99b56c923de187d20544` (branch `arena/ba6a87b3-clain-filemanagaer`).
Supporting files: `audit_v4_video/` (`patches/`, `repro/`, `harness/`).

## 1. خلاصه (فارسی)

۱. هفت نقص تأیید‌شده پیدا شد؛ هر هفت مورد بازتولید شده و برای هر کدام اصلاحیه‌ای با `git diff` ساخته و آزموده شده است (پس از هر هفت اصلاح: `Results: 557/557 passed, 0 failures`).
۲. جدی‌ترین: یک SPS خراب H.264 (فایل ۸٫۸ کیلوبایتی از فازر) در ساخت ۳۲ بیتی باعث SIGSEGV می‌شود، چون `picSizeInMbs * sizeof` در ۳۲ بیت سرریز می‌کند (VID4-01).
۳. یک فایل MP4 سی‌وشش بایتی `Video_Open` را برای همیشه در حلقه نگه می‌دارد (VID4-02).
۴. در SimpleVideo پرش (seek) در فایل‌های بلند یا MP4/MOV با صدای PCM صدا را به جای غلط می‌برد: سرریز ضرب ۳۲ بیتی (VID4-03).
۵. در SimpleVideo صدای MP3، PCM هشت‌بیتی و PCM بیست‌وچهار بیتی کاملاً ساکت پخش می‌شود (VID4-04).
۶. سرنخ W3 تأیید شد: نرخ فریم به عدد صحیح گرد می‌شود؛ فایل ۲۹٫۹۷ در ۳۰ دقیقه ۱٫۷۸ ثانیه و فایل ۱۲٫۵ فریم در ۶۰ ثانیه ۲٫۲ ثانیه از صدا جلو می‌افتد (VID4-05).
۷. بلوک FLAC بزرگ‌تر از بافر بی‌صدا بریده می‌شود و SimpleVideo نیمی از صدای FLAC ‎۴۴٫۱ کیلوهرتز را دور می‌ریزد (VID4-06)؛ پوشه‌خوانِ پخش‌کننده فایل‌های mkv/webm/flv را نمی‌بیند (VID4-07).
۸. کار ۱: VID-01 و M-01 تا M-08 همگی «اصلاح شده»؛ با برداشتن هر اصلاح آزمونش شکست می‌خورد، جز دو شکاف آزمون (ADPCM مایکروسافت در ویدیو، BT.709 تمام‌دامنه).
۹. مقایسه با ffmpeg: ‎۲۰۸ فایل از ۳۸۰ فایل کاملاً درست؛ بقیه یا ساخته نشدند یا رد شدند و با بخش ۷ تطبیق کامل داده نشدند. W1 و W4 نقص نیستند.
۱۰. انجام‌نشده‌ها (بخش ۱۰) زیادند: `VideoFile_Repair`، `Video_Convert`، فایل‌های بدون `idx1`، تزریق خطا، `SetSwap16`، و اندازه‌گیری پشته روی ARM.
۱۱. هیچ آزمونی به مجموعه آزمون افزوده نشد؛ بازتولیدها به شکل فایل و فرمان تحویل شده‌اند.

## 2. Audit record

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Machine | Linux x86-64, 2 CPUs, 3 GB RAM, gcc 12.2.0, GNU Make 4.3, Python 3.11 |
| Reference | ffmpeg 9.0.2 (imageio-ffmpeg binary; libx264, libmp3lame, libvorbis, libopus) and PyAV |
| Not available | `ffprobe`, `clang`, `cppcheck`, `afl-fuzz`, `gdb`, a 32-bit gcc runtime, an ARM libc |
| Substitutes | PyAV / `ffmpeg` for counts and durations; own mutational fuzzer `harness/vfuzz.c` (ASan+UBSan+LSan); `backtrace()` + `addr2line`; `python -m ziglang cc -target x86-linux-musl` for 32-bit builds (no sanitizer in that build) |
| Baseline at 48a00bc | `make test -j2` → `Results: 557/557 passed, 0 failures`; `make m32` 558/558 and `make tsan` 562/562 (run once at the start) |
| After all seven fixes | fresh clone at 48a00bc, the seven patches applied in order with `git apply --check` + `git apply`, `make test -j2` → `Results: 557/557 passed, 0 failures` |
| Not re-run after the fixes | `make m32`, `make tsan`, `make asan`, `videohooks`, `saveexamplestest` |
| GitHub | nothing was pushed, committed remotely, or commented on |

Harnesses (sources in `audit_v4_video/harness/`):

- `vdump <file> [-v out.rgb] [-a out.pcm] [-c cap]` — opens a file through `Video_Open`, decodes every picture and every audio packet, prints counts and return codes.
- `vfuzz <seed-dir> <iters> <seed>` — mutates one of 66 ffmpeg-made seed files (AVI, MP4, MOV, MKV, FLV with MJPEG / H.264 and nine audio codecs), then opens, reads, decodes and seeks.
- `svsim` — includes `examples/simple_video.h` on the HAL stub with a simulated clock and a simulated I2S DMA. The clip (`gen_av.py`) carries its picture number in the pixels and its sample index in the PCM, so every presented picture and every played buffer is identified and the A/V offset is measured, not estimated.
- `diffrun.py`/`gen1.py` (380 container × codec × channel files against ffmpeg), `genlong.py` (W1), `col.py` (W4), `mut.py` (Job 1 mutations).

The diffs printed in section 4 are the `git diff` output with the carriage returns removed for display (the sources are CRLF); the files in `patches/` are the unmodified output and are the ones to apply. The patches in `patches/` are cumulative: apply them in numerical order of the file names (`VID4-01_mp4_box_size64` … `VID4-07_h264_sps_size`). The file names carry the order in which I found the defects; the finding IDs below are ordered by severity. The mapping is in each finding.

## 4. Confirmed findings

### VID4-01 — H.264 SPS with an absurd picture size: 32-bit wrap, SIGSEGV on a 32-bit build (Critical)

**Where.** `h264bsdDecodeSeqParamSet`, `third_party/h264bsd/src/h264bsd_seq_param_set.c:254` and `:259`: `picWidthInMbs = value + 1`, `picHeightInMbs = value + 1`, each from an unbounded Exp-Golomb value. `h264bsdActivateParamSets`, `h264bsd_storage.c:340-342` multiplies them into the `u32` `picSizeInMbs`; `:357-358` allocates `picSizeInMbs * sizeof(mbStorage_t)` through `ALLOCATE` (`h264bsd_util.h:188`, `FM_Alloc((count) * sizeof(type))`). Third-party code, reached through `Video_Open` + `Video_DecodeFrame` with a file.

**Observed.** Input `repro/vid4_h264_sps_huge.mkv` (8805 bytes, written by the fuzzer, round 14 of run 2).

- 64-bit ASan build: `timeout 60 vdump.asan /c3_14_1412147829.mkv -v /tmp/o.rgb` →
  `==41733==ERROR: AddressSanitizer: allocator is out of memory trying to allocate 0x8000008800 bytes`, stack `FM_Alloc src/fm_alloc.c:153` ← `h264bsdActivateParamSets third_party/h264bsd/src/h264bsd_storage.c:357` ← `h264bsdDecode h264bsd_decoder.c:395`.
- 32-bit build (zig cc, `x86-linux-musl`, `-g -O1`): `vdump.m32 /c3_14_1412147829.mkv -v /tmp/o.rgb` prints `open=0` and the process ends with exit status 139 (SIGSEGV). A second fuzzer file (`c3_47_526153694.mov`) gives the same two results.
- The same 32-bit binary decodes a sound file: `vdump.m32 /mkv_h264_flac_2.mkv` → `video: decoded=75 notready=0 bad=0`.

In 32 bits the requested size `0x8000008800` is `0x8800` (34 816 bytes): the allocation succeeds and is far smaller than `picSizeInMbs` entries. I did not locate the faulting instruction (no debugger, no 32-bit sanitizer); the crash itself is observed, the first out-of-bounds access is not pinned down.

**Fix** (`patches/VID4-07_h264_sps_size.diff`): refuse an SPS whose size is zero or above 36 864 macroblocks (MaxFS of level 5.1).

```diff
diff --git a/Clain_FileManager/FileManager_lib/third_party/h264bsd/src/h264bsd_seq_param_set.c b/Clain_FileManager/FileManager_lib/third_party/h264bsd/src/h264bsd_seq_param_set.c
index 2f1db82..bbf277c 100644
--- a/Clain_FileManager/FileManager_lib/third_party/h264bsd/src/h264bsd_seq_param_set.c
+++ b/Clain_FileManager/FileManager_lib/third_party/h264bsd/src/h264bsd_seq_param_set.c
@@ -258,6 +258,18 @@ u32 h264bsdDecodeSeqParamSet(strmData_t *pStrmData, seqParamSet_t *pSeqParamSet)
         return(tmp);
     pSeqParamSet->picHeightInMbs = value + 1;
 
+    /* A picture larger than any level allows (MaxFS of level 5.1 is
+     * 36864 macroblocks) is damage: the sizes computed from it wrap in
+     * 32 bits and the buffers allocated for it are then too small. */
+    if (pSeqParamSet->picWidthInMbs == 0 ||
+        pSeqParamSet->picHeightInMbs == 0 ||
+        pSeqParamSet->picWidthInMbs > 36864u ||
+        pSeqParamSet->picHeightInMbs >
+            36864u / pSeqParamSet->picWidthInMbs)
+    {
+        return(HANTRO_NOK);
+    }
+
     /* frame_mbs_only_flag, shall be 1 for baseline profile */
     tmp = h264bsdGetBits(pStrmData, 1);
     if (tmp == END_OF_STREAM)
```

**After the fix.** 32-bit build: both files → exit status 0, `video: decoded=0 notready=0 bad=4 firstbad=-17` (and `bad=6` for the `.mov`); `mkv_h264_flac_2.mkv` still `decoded=75 ... bad=0`. `make test` → `Results: 557/557 passed, 0 failures`.
**Test included:** no (reproducer file only).

### VID4-02 — MP4 box with a 64-bit size below 16: `Video_Open` never returns (High)

**Where.** `mp4_box_at`, `src/fm_video.c:1312-1348`. For a 32-bit size field of 1 the 64-bit size is read and `data = pos + 16`; the only check is line 1343, `if (box->data > end || box->size > end - pos)`. A 64-bit size of 0…15 passes. `mp4_walk` then does `pos = box.start + box.size` (line 1373) and stays on the same box; the same statement is in `mp4_find_nested` (1426) and in `mp4_visual_object_type`.

**Observed.** `repro/vid4_mp4_size64_zero.mp4`, 36 bytes: `000000146674797069736f6d0000000069736f6d00000001667265650000000000000000`. `timeout 10 vdump /z.mp4` → exit status 124. Found by the fuzzer after about 11 s (`HANG in f.mp4`); stack by `addr2line`: `video_read_at fm_video.c:329` ← `mp4_walk 1361` ← `Video_Open 4565`.

**Fix** (`patches/VID4-01_mp4_box_size64.diff`):

```diff
diff --git a/Clain_FileManager/FileManager_lib/src/fm_video.c b/Clain_FileManager/FileManager_lib/src/fm_video.c
index 7a4e266..dd38d9a 100644
--- a/Clain_FileManager/FileManager_lib/src/fm_video.c
+++ b/Clain_FileManager/FileManager_lib/src/fm_video.c
@@ -1340,7 +1340,8 @@ static FM_Result mp4_box_at(FM_Video *v, uint64_t pos, uint64_t end,
     {
         box->size = end - pos;
     }
-    if (box->data > end || box->size > end - pos)
+    if (box->data > end || box->size > end - pos ||
+        box->size < box->data - pos)     /* shorter than its own header */
     {
         return FM_ERR_CORRUPTED;
     }
```

**After the fix.** `vdump /z.mp4` → `open=-17`, exit status 1. `make test` → `Results: 557/557 passed, 0 failures`. All later fuzzing ran with this fix.
**Test included:** no (reproducer file only).

### VID4-03 — Seek: 32-bit product overflows, sound resumes at the wrong place (High)

**Where.** `simple_video_sync_audio`, `top_api/simple_video.h:2062`: `idx = (simple_video_now_elapsed() * sv.audio.sample_count) / dur_ms;` — both operands are `uint32_t`. The product wraps when position in ms × packet count reaches 2^32. For MP4/MOV PCM the packet count is the number of sample frames (1 323 000 for 60 s at 22 050 Hz), so every seek beyond about 3.2 s wraps; for AVI it takes a long file.

**Observed** (`svsim`, picture and sound positions read from the data itself):

- 60 s MOV, MJPEG + PCM 22 050 Hz, `svsim -fps 25 -srate 22050 -seek 5:30`: `seek_count=1 seek_sound_ms=787`; `wall 12.066 s  sound 7.846 s  picture 37.040 s  offset +29193.6 ms`. Identical figures for the same clip as `.mp4`.
- 30 min AVI (29.97 fps, 19 380 packets), `-seek 5:1500`: `seek_sound_ms=170263`; `wall 7.607 s  sound 173.035 s  picture 1504.069 s  offset +1331034.1 ms`.
- Not affected: 600 s AVI, seek to 400 s → offset +47.3 ms; seek to 590 s → −7.2 ms.

**Fix** (`patches/VID4-02_seek_sound_overflow.diff`):

```diff
diff --git a/Clain_FileManager/FileManager_lib/examples/simple_video.h b/Clain_FileManager/FileManager_lib/examples/simple_video.h
index 525ae8a..994ed3b 100644
--- a/Clain_FileManager/FileManager_lib/examples/simple_video.h
+++ b/Clain_FileManager/FileManager_lib/examples/simple_video.h
@@ -2059,7 +2059,8 @@ static FM_Result simple_video_sync_audio(void)
     {
         return FM_OK;
     }
-    idx = (simple_video_now_elapsed() * sv.audio.sample_count) / dur_ms;
+    idx = (uint32_t)(((uint64_t)simple_video_now_elapsed() *
+                      sv.audio.sample_count) / dur_ms);
     if (idx >= sv.audio.sample_count)
     {
         idx = sv.audio.sample_count - 1u;
diff --git a/Clain_FileManager/FileManager_lib/top_api/simple_video.h b/Clain_FileManager/FileManager_lib/top_api/simple_video.h
index 4dc6cbf..3150c8a 100644
--- a/Clain_FileManager/FileManager_lib/top_api/simple_video.h
+++ b/Clain_FileManager/FileManager_lib/top_api/simple_video.h
@@ -2059,7 +2059,8 @@ static FM_Result simple_video_sync_audio(void)
     {
         return FM_OK;
     }
-    idx = (simple_video_now_elapsed() * sv.audio.sample_count) / dur_ms;
+    idx = (uint32_t)(((uint64_t)simple_video_now_elapsed() *
+                      sv.audio.sample_count) / dur_ms);
     if (idx >= sv.audio.sample_count)
     {
         idx = sv.audio.sample_count - 1u;
```

**After the fix.** MOV and MP4: `seek_sound_ms=30004`, `sound 37.064 s  picture 37.040 s  offset -23.9 ms`. 30 min AVI: `seek_sound_ms=1499984`, `sound 1504.096 s  picture 1504.069 s  offset -26.5 ms`. `make test` (with fixes 01–03 of the patch order) → `Results: 557/557 passed, 0 failures`.
**Test included:** no. The existing seek test (`tools/save_examples_host_test.c`, `[5b]`) seeks in a short clip and cannot reach the wrap.

### VID4-04 — SimpleVideo plays MP3, 8-bit PCM and 24-bit PCM tracks as silence (High)

**Where.** Clip start, `top_api/simple_video.h:2134-2146`: PCM is accepted only with `bits_per_sample` 16 or 8, MP3 / ADPCM / A-law / µ-law only with `bits_per_sample == 16`. `Video_GetAudioInfo` reports `bits=0` for MP3 (in AVI, MP4, MKV, FLV, MOV alike) and 24 for 24-bit PCM, with `decodable=1`. A second check at `:1496-1498` refuses everything that is not 16, which also removes the 8-bit PCM that line 2136 let through — against the comment at 2138–2139 ("8-bit PCM is up-converted … so I2S can play it") and against `fm_video.h` ("PCM is always handed out as 16-bit").

**Observed** (10 s clips, `svsim -fps 25 -srate <rate>`):

| Clip | Before | After the fix |
|---|---|---|
| AVI, MP3 44.1 kHz | `halves played=53 silent=53`, `data=0 … monitor=0` | `silent=0`, `data=54 (884736 samples)` |
| MP4, MP3 44.1 kHz | `halves played=53 silent=53`, `data=0` | `silent=0`, `data=54 (882000 samples)` |
| AVI, MP3 mono | `halves played=53 silent=53`, `data=0` | `silent=0`, `data=54 (884736 samples)` |
| AVI, `pcm_s24le` | `halves played=26 silent=26`, `data=0` | `silent=0 with-source-sound=26`, `data=27 (441000 samples, first src 0, end src 220500, discontinuities 0)` |
| AVI, `pcm_u8` | `dma starts=0 … halves played=0`, `data=0` | `halves played=26 silent=0`, `data=27 (441000 samples)` |
| AVI, PCM 16-bit (control) | `silent=0 with-source-sound=53`, offset −49.9…0 ms | unchanged |

Pictures are complete in every row (250/250). No test in the suite plays an MP3 track through SimpleVideo.

**Fix** (`patches/VID4-03_silent_tracks.diff`): rely on `decodable` instead of the source width.

```diff
diff --git a/Clain_FileManager/FileManager_lib/examples/simple_video.h b/Clain_FileManager/FileManager_lib/examples/simple_video.h
index 994ed3b..fef481f 100644
--- a/Clain_FileManager/FileManager_lib/examples/simple_video.h
+++ b/Clain_FileManager/FileManager_lib/examples/simple_video.h
@@ -1494,8 +1494,7 @@ static FM_Result sv_i2s_start(void)
     }
     rate = sv.audio.sample_rate;
     if (!sv_i2s_rate_ok(rate) ||
-        (sv.audio.channels != 1u && sv.audio.channels != 2u) ||
-        sv.audio.bits_per_sample != 16u)
+        (sv.audio.channels != 1u && sv.audio.channels != 2u))
     {
         FM_Log("SimpleVideo: PCM %lu Hz %uch %ubit not I2S-playable\n",
                (unsigned long)rate, (unsigned)sv.audio.channels,
@@ -2133,8 +2132,7 @@ static FM_Result simple_video_prime_item(uint32_t index)
         {
             sv.has_audio = FM_TRUE;
             if (sv.audio.codec == FM_VIDEO_AUDIO_PCM &&
-                (sv.audio.bits_per_sample == 16u ||
-                 sv.audio.bits_per_sample == 8u))
+                sv.audio.decodable)
             {
                 /* 8-bit PCM is up-converted to 16-bit in fm_video.c
```

### VID4-05 — Frame rate kept as a rounded integer: the picture runs away from the sound (Medium)

**Where.** `FM_VideoInfo.fps` is a whole number, filled to the nearest integer in `src/fm_video.c:1147` (AVI), `2888` (MP4), `3866` (MKV), `4438` (FLV). `SimpleVideo_Poll` schedules with it: `top_api/simple_video.h:2836`, `want = (simple_video_now_elapsed() * fps) / 1000u`; `Player_Seek` (`src/fm_player.c:408`) and `simple_video_duration` use it too. The comment at `fm_video.c:1140-1145` describes the cure of truncation (29 → 30); the remaining 0.1 % for NTSC rates and up to 6.7 % for half rates is the defect. The library's own writer accepts `fps` / `fps_den` (`fm_video.h:353-354`), so it can write files its player mis-times; I did not run that path.

**Observed.** `vdump`: 30000/1001 → `fps=30`, 24000/1001 → `fps=24`, 12.5 → `fps=13`, 7.5 → `fps=8`, identical in AVI, MP4, MKV, FLV, MOV. `svsim`, simulated clock exact, I2S rate exact:

- 30 min AVI, 29.97 fps, 53 946 pictures: `ended at sim 1798.214 s`; `wall 750.476 s … offset +714.8 ms`; `wall 1797.788 s  sound 1797.782 s  picture 1799.564 s  offset +1782.7 ms`.
- 60 s AVI, 12.5 fps: `ended at sim 57.707 s`; `wall 57.222 s  sound 57.214 s  picture 59.440 s  offset +2226.0 ms`.

**Fix** (`patches/VID4-04_exact_frame_rate.diff`): `FM_VideoInfo` gains `fps_num` / `fps_den` (appended; `fps` unchanged), filled by the four parsers and defaulted in `Video_Open`; `SimpleVideo_Poll`, `simple_video_duration`, `Player_Seek` and the position getter use the fraction.

### VID4-06 — FLAC block larger than the buffer is cut with `FM_OK` (Medium)

**Where.** `flac_decode`, `src/fm_flac.c:112`, reads at most `out_count / ch` frames of the block and returns `FM_OK`; `video_audio_decode_packet`, `src/fm_video.c:5925-5927`, then advances to the next packet. The rest of the block is gone, no error. The contract in `fm_video.h` sets no minimum `cap` for FLAC. SimpleVideo's buffer is 8192 bytes (`top_api/simple_video.h:872`, `include/fm_board_config.h:531`) and its comment (867–870) says "nothing is dropped"; an ffmpeg FLAC track at 44.1 kHz stereo has 4096-frame blocks = 16 384 bytes.

**Observed.** `repro/flac4096.mkv` (33 blocks; ffmpeg decodes 529 200 bytes):

| `vdump -c` | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| 4096 | `calls=33 ok=33 bad=0 … bytes=135168` | `calls=0 ok=0 bad=1 firstbad=-16` |
| 8192 | `calls=33 ok=33 bad=0 … bytes=267056` | `calls=0 ok=0 bad=1 firstbad=-16` |
| 16384 | `calls=33 ok=33 bad=0 … bytes=529200` | same, output equal to ffmpeg's (`cmp`) |

SimpleVideo, 10 s MP4 + FLAC 44.1 kHz stereo: before `halves played=53 silent=26 with-source-sound=27`, `data=27 (442368 samples, first src 0, end src 434176, discontinuities 26)`, sound up to 4859.5 ms ahead of the picture. After: `silent=0 with-source-sound=53`, `data=54 (882000 samples, first src 0, end src 441000, discontinuities 0)`, `min -49.4 ms  max +0.0 ms`.

**Fix** (`patches/VID4-05_flac_block_cut.diff`): the decoder reports `FM_ERR_TOO_LARGE` when the block holds more than fits, the video layer then takes nothing, and SimpleVideo's default buffer becomes 18 432 bytes (one 4608-frame stereo block; +10 KB of `.dma_buffer`). A FLAC stream with larger blocks is then refused aloud instead of being thinned. `src/fm_flac.c` belongs to the audio part; the change there is five lines.


### VID4-07 — Folder scan ignores `.mkv`, `.webm`, `.flv` (Low)

**Where.** `is_video_path`, `src/fm_player.c:52-57`: `avi`, `mp4`, `m4v`, `mov`, `fmrv`. README lines 72, 93 and 473 list Matroska/WebM and FLV as supported containers; section 7 of the prompt documents the omission only for `.m4a`.

**Observed.** A folder holding only `a.mkv`: `svsim -v -v` → `SimpleVideo: playlist 0 in /videos`, `begin/start=-3`. Same for `a.flv`.

**Fix** (`patches/VID4-06_player_scan_mkv_flv.diff`):

```diff

            ext_is(path, "m4v") || ext_is(path, "mov") ||
-           ext_is(path, "fmrv");
+           ext_is(path, "mkv") || ext_is(path, "webm") ||
+           ext_is(path, "flv") || ext_is(path, "fmrv");
 }
 
 static FM_Bool kind_wants(FM_PlayerKind kinds, const char *path)
```

**After the fix.** MKV + FLAC: `pictures: presented=250 distinct=250`, `data=54 (882000 samples, first src 0, end src 441000, discontinuities 0)`; MKV + Vorbis and FLV + H.264 + MP3 play to the end with sound. `make test` → `Results: 557/557 passed, 0 failures`. `src/fm_media.c:48-51` has a similar list (avi/mp4/mov); I did not examine or change it.
**Test included:** no.

## 5. Suspicions (not confirmed as defects)

| # | What | What stopped me |
|---|---|---|
| S-1 | `third_party/dr_flac/dr_flac.h:3063/3064`, `drflac__calculate_prediction_32`: UBSan `signed integer overflow: 115000920 * 385 cannot be represented in type 'int'` on fuzzed FLAC tracks (4 inputs, `c2_*`). | Undefined behaviour is observed; a wrong result or memory error is not. Third-party. |
| S-2 | `third_party/h264bsd/src/h264bsd_reconstruct.c:1540` and `:1759`: UBSan `left shift of negative value -10` / `-768` in inter prediction on fuzzed streams (3 inputs, `c3_36`, `c3_43`, `c3_54`). | Same: UB observed, no consequence shown. Third-party. |
| S-3 | MP3 with `cap` below 4608 loses data silently (`avi_mp3.avi`: 216 576 bytes complete, `cap` 4095 → 192 418, 1024 → 48 128, all `ok=47 bad=0`). The header asks for "at least" 4608, so the caller broke the contract; the library still answers `FM_OK`. In `mkv_mp3_delay.mkv`, `cap` 2…64 gives `calls=0 bytes=0 last=-15`. | Documented precondition; not judged a defect. |
| S-4 | MP4 Vorbis / Opus length: library 131 928 vs ffmpeg 132 300 samples (Vorbis), 144 000 vs 144 648 (Opus). The file's edit list says 131 928; the library follows it. | Which of the two is right for the end trim was not settled. |
| S-5 | Picture timing follows `HAL_GetTick`, the sound follows the I2S clock; nothing ties them. With the I2S 0.2 % fast (22 094 Hz for 22 050) and VID4-05 fixed: `wall 1796.061 s  sound 1799.639 s  picture 1796.061 s  offset -3578.4 ms` after 30 min. | Measured, but whether a 0.2 % error occurs on the board's PLL was not established, and no fix was written. |
| S-6 | Two fuzzer stops reported as `HANG` (`c3_7`, `c3_60`): `c3_7` replays in 2.3 s uninstrumented and 10.5 s under ASan (the fuzzer's alarm is 10 s). 2.3 s for a 9 KB file is slow. | `c3_60` not replayed; cause of the slowness not looked for. |
| S-7 | `SimpleVideo_Poll` line 2836, `elapsed_ms * fps` in 32 bits wraps after 2^32 / fps ms (39.7 h at 30 fps). | Arithmetic only, not run. Removed by the VID4-05 patch. |

## 6. Verdict on the earlier findings (Job 1)

Method: `mut.py` removes or breaks each fix in a copy of the tree, then `make test -j2`.

| ID | Verdict | Mutation | `make test` with the fix removed |
|---|---|---|---|
| VID-01 | fixed | depth check `if (depth + 1u >= MP4_MAX_NEST)` → `if (0)` | `556/557`, `VID-01: MP4 boxes nested past the limit are refused ... FAIL` |
| M-01 | fixed | `mp3dec_init` on every call | `553/557`, four MP3 tests fail (`the tone is whole`, …) |
| M-02 (IMA) | fixed | IMA nibble order swapped | `554/557`, `IMA stereo gives ffmpeg's PCM, byte for byte ... FAIL` + 2 |
| M-02 (Microsoft) | fixed, **video test blind** | Microsoft nibble order swapped | video test `ADPCM in AVI: Microsoft ADPCM gives ffmpeg's PCM, byte for byte ... PASS`; only three WAV tests of the audio part fail (`554/557`) |
| M-03 | fixed | `vorbis_private_split` always fails | `551/557`, six Vorbis tests fail |
| M-04 | fixed | matrix forced to BT.601 full range / to BT.601 video range | `555/557` / `556/557` |
| M-04, BT.709 full range | fixed, **no test** | row 3 of `k_yuv_matrix` replaced by row 2 | `557/557` |
| M-05 | fixed | `mp4_visual_object_type` returns 0 | `556/557`, `MJPEG in MP4 … FAIL: codec 4, 64x48` |
| M-06 | fixed | `twos` treated as little-endian | `556/557`, `big-endian PCM (twos) is handed out little-endian ... FAIL` |
| M-07 | fixed | `mp4_audio_edit` no-op / `mkv_group_discard` returns 0 | `555/557` / `551/557` |
| M-08 | fixed | `video_in_file` always true | `555/557`, both "4 GB in the index is not allocated" tests fail |

Correctness against ffmpeg (not only "the test notices"): Microsoft and IMA ADPCM, MP3, Vorbis, PCM widths and H.264 colour are inside the 208 files of the differential run that match ffmpeg (section 9).

VID-01 beyond the reported file: ten crafted files with 5000 levels (MP4 `moov`/`trak`/… chain, MP4 `udta` chain, AVI `LIST` in `LIST` under `movi` and under `hdrl`, MKV `BlockGroup`, `Segment`, `Tracks`/`TrackEntry` chains, FLV script objects and strict arrays) and MP4 chains of 7, 8, 9 levels, each run with `ulimit -s 256` on the plain and the ASan build: every one is refused (`open=-17`, one `open=-12`), no crash, no sanitizer report. A legitimate file cannot nest the nine container types of `mp4_find_nested` deeper than five; all ffmpeg-written MP4 / MOV files of the differential run open. No phone-camera or QuickTime-written file was available.

The M-08 class is **not** closed for the H.264 decoder: VID4-01 is a size from the stream that becomes an allocation.

## 7. Answers to the open leads

| Lead | Answer | How checked |
|---|---|---|
| W1 clips longer than `FM_VIDEO_MAX_FRAMES` | Not a defect at 4096. | 28 files of 4095 / 4096 / 4097 / 40 960 frames × 7 container-codec pairs: every picture decoded and matched (`bad=0`, worst PSNR 46.9 dB H.264, 48.1 dB MJPEG), audio sample counts equal to ffmpeg (`L40960_mjpeg_pcm_s16le.avi`: `A samples lib=36126720 ref=36126720 maxdiff=0`). 300 random seeks each on AVI 4097 / 40 960, MOV 40 960, MKV 40 960: 0 wrong pictures, slowest seek 160.2 ms (host). Not checked at 1024 or 8192, nor under ASan. |
| W2 static RAM of `g_video` | Measured. 64-bit host: 153 888 (1024 frames), 547 104 (4096), 1 071 392 (8192), 38 472 (1 slot × 1024). 32-bit x86: 120 624 / 415 536 / 808 752 / 30 156. README's 546 688 is 416 bytes below the measured 64-bit value; "≈ 38 KB" holds. | `nm -S` on `fm_video.o` built with `-DFM_VIDEO_MAX_FRAMES=…` / `-DFM_MAX_VIDEO=1`; 32-bit with zig cc. ARM not built; whether the README states the 8192 figure was not checked. |
| W3 A/V drift | Confirmed defect → VID4-05 (+1782.7 ms in 30 min at 29.97 fps). Inexact I2S rate: −3578.4 ms in 30 min at +0.2 %, see S-5. | `svsim`, 30 min clip. |
| W4 BT.709 full range | Decoder correct; no test → section 8. | 96 files (8 VUI cases × 3 sizes × 4 containers), library RGB against the documented matrix in numpy: 96/96 max difference ≤ 1. Mutation M-04c passes the suite. |
| W5 ARM stack depth | Could not settle. Largest single frames on x86-64, `-Os -fstack-usage`: `h264bsdInterpolateMidHorQuarter` 1976, `…MidVerQuarter` 1952, `…MidHalf` 1944, `ConcealMb` 672, `mp4_parse` 592, `h264bsdDecodeSliceData` 576, `mkv_parse` 384 bytes. | No ARM libc for the cross compiler; no call-chain sum made. |

## 8. Tests that would not notice

1. `ADPCM in AVI: Microsoft ADPCM gives ffmpeg's PCM, byte for byte` — the vector `avi_adpcm_ms.avi` has identical channels (11 132 frames, 0 with L ≠ R), so swapped nibbles / swapped channels leave the CRC unchanged. Shown by mutation M-02b.
2. No H.264 vector with BT.709 full range (mutation M-04c: 557/557).
3. No test plays an MP3, 8-bit or 24-bit track through SimpleVideo and checks that sound comes out (VID4-04 lived there).
4. Seek tests use short AVI clips; none checks the sound position after a seek in MP4 / MOV or beyond the 32-bit wrap (VID4-03).
5. No clip with a fractional frame rate is timed (VID4-05); no FLAC vector with blocks larger than the read buffer (VID4-06).
6. No test builds the H.264 decoder for 32 bits with an oversized SPS (VID4-01); `make m32` passes at 558/558.

## 9. Examined and found sound

- **Decoding against ffmpeg**, 380 generated files (5 containers × MJPEG / H.264 × audio codecs × mono / stereo), `cap` 65 536: 208 files have 75/75 pictures at PSNR ≥ 40 dB, equal audio sample count and maximum sample difference ≤ 2. 122 could not be written by ffmpeg. The remaining 50 are refusals or "no audio track" (`open=-12`: AVI + big-endian / signed 8-bit PCM / Opus; `open=-17`: MP4 + 8-bit PCM / A-law / µ-law / ADPCM, MOV + Opus / FLAC, MKV + f32be / s8, FLV + 24 / 32-bit / BE PCM / ADPCM / Vorbis; no track: AVI + Vorbis / FLAC, MOV + ADPCM / Vorbis, FLV + Opus / FLAC). No wrong output among them; I did **not** finish matching each group against section 7 and the README table, and `open=-17` for a whole file because of its audio track deserves a second look. AAC rows were not reviewed.
- **Small buffers**: 20 `cap` values per file under ASan+UBSan: no sanitizer report, no write past `cap`.
- **Fuzzing** (own mutational fuzzer, ASan + UBSan + LSan, 66 seeds, MP4 fix applied): run 1, 300 000 executions; run 2, 60 rounds of up to 20 000 (53 complete = 1 060 000; 7 stopped early). Per 20 000: about `open_ok=11400 frames_ok=53000 audio_ok=136000`. Results: VID4-02 (before the fix), VID4-01, S-1, S-2, S-6; no heap error, no leak, no contract violation in the library's own code. Coverage of one 20 000-execution run (gcov, `-O0`): `fm_video.c` 78.27 % of 3207 lines, 85.32 % of 2807 branches executed; `fm_h264.c` 77.43 % / 82.48 %; `fm_mjpeg.c` 89.41 % / 100 %; `fm_mediacache.c` and `fm_player.c` 0 % (not reached by this harness). No FLV + MJPEG and no FMRV seeds.
- **SimpleVideo timing**, AVI MJPEG + PCM 25 fps: 500/500 pictures in order, 882 000 samples continuous, offset −49.9…0 ms; after seeks forward, backward, to 0, two seeks 10 ms apart, pause then seek, and with a 9 ms poll period: one sound discontinuity per seek and `seek_count` correct in every run; offset after the seek within −48…+47 ms for single seeks at a 1 ms poll period, −69.6…−53.2 ms after the two close seeks, and −275.6…−48.0 ms at the 9 ms poll period (where it is −202.5…−98.2 ms before the seek as well). Seek past the end ends the item with `seek_count=0`. `SimpleVideo_Info().frame_index` equals picture number + 1 at every presentation (the meaning `examples_save_media.h:765-768` relies on). Mono tracks, ADPCM and µ-law play with sound.
- `Video_SeekAudio` on MOV PCM lands on the requested sample (shown by VID4-03 after its fix); my earlier lead about it was a wrong comparison.

## 10. Not examined

- Job 2: `SimpleVideo_SetAudioOutput` switching during play and its contract (3206–3232), playing with no callbacks / callback-only output, `SetSwap16`, `Next` / `Prev` / `Stop` / autoplay sequences — the harness has the options, the runs were not made.
- AVI files from the new writer, files without `idx1`, `VideoFile_Repair`, `Video_Convert`.
- The `u32` type change in h264bsd beyond what fuzzing and VID4-01 show.
- `fm_mediacache.c`, `examples/hw_jpeg_video.h`, `examples/play_from_flash.h`, `board/app_video_hooks.h`, `test/video_hooks_test.c`, `tools/gen_test_videos.py`: not read.
- Fault injection (I/O errors, allocation failures), thread-safety, `make m32` / `tsan` / `asan` after the fixes.
- H.264 Main / High refusal, MJPEG layouts picojpeg cannot hold, FMRV, FLV + MJPEG.
- `FM_VIDEO_MAX_FRAMES` 1024 and 8192 behaviour; ARM builds of any kind.
- `ReportV2.txt` lines 1089–1265 were not re-read for this round; the Job 1 table follows the prompt's list.
- The reproducers were run before and after each fix in working copies; in the final fresh clone only the patch application and `make test` were repeated.

## 11. Outside my part

- `src/fm_flac.c` (`flac_decode` returns `FM_OK` for a partial block) — audio part; fixed here because the video path depends on it (VID4-06).
- `src/fm_media.c:48-51` has its own short list of video extensions.
- Mutation M-02b fails three `WAV Microsoft ADPCM` tests: the audio part's vectors do notice what the video vector misses.

```
# builds

make libfilemanager.a CC="python -m ziglang cc -target x86-linux-musl" AR="python -m ziglang ar" CFLAGS_EXTRA="-g -O1" FM_USE_OPUS_LIB=1   # 32-bit tree
sh harness/build.sh vdump.c ; sh harness/b.sh <tree> <suffix>   # vdump / svsim

# reproducers
timeout 10 vdump /vid4_mp4_size64_zero.mp4                        # VID4-02
vdump.m32 /vid4_h264_sps_huge.mkv -v /tmp/o.rgb ; echo $?         # VID4-01
python gen_av.py videos/a.mov 25 60 22050 ; svsim -fps 25 -srate 22050 -seek 5:30 -maxt 80      # VID4-03
python gen_av.py videos/a.avi 25 10 44100 -c:a libmp3lame ; svsim -fps 25 -srate 44100 -maxt 30 # VID4-04
python gen_av.py videos/a.avi 30000/1001 1800 22050 ; svsim -fps 30000/1001 -srate 22050        # VID4-05
vdump /flac4096.mkv -c 8192 -a /tmp/x.pcm                         # VID4-06
python gen_av.py videos/a.mkv 25 10 44100 -c:a flac ; svsim -fps 25 -srate 44100 -v -v          # VID4-07

# Job 1, fuzzing, coverage
python mut.py <ID> ; make test -j2
sh fzloop.sh 3 vfuzz.asan.r1 60
vfuzz.cov seeds 20000 300001 ; gcov -b -o src src/fm_video.c
```





# AUDIT_SAVE_V2 — external audit V2: saving and recording (audio, video) and how the parts work together

Commit audited: `48a00bc613cac4eb407f99b56c923de187d20544` ("Save / record: fixes for the findings of the external audit (AUDIT_SAVE_V1)"), 2026-11-12.
Report scope: `Clain_FileManager/FileManager_lib/` (paths below are relative to it unless they start with `Core/`).

---

## 1. خلاصه (Summary, Persian)

۱. این دور **یک نقص تأییدشده** یافت — SAV2-02، شدت S4 — و **دو مورد مصرف‌شده/رد‌شده** که در بخش ۵ آمده‌اند. **هیچ S1، S2 یا S3 تأییدشده‌ای پیدا نشد.**
۲. مهم‌ترین نکتهٔ این دور آن است که یکی از دو نامزدِ اصلیِ «از دست رفتن داده» **با مستندات پوشش داده شده و نقص نیست**: راهنما (`docs/GUIDE_FA.md:460`) صریح می‌گوید «کمتر از ۲۵۶ کیلوبایت = فایل خالی»، و آزمون نشان داد ADPCM و WAV هر دو دقیقاً همین کار را می‌کنند. آنچه من در ابتدا نقص پنداشتم (هدر تا اولین checkpoint اعداد صفر دارد) همان قرارداد مستند است؛ بخش ۵ با اعداد توضیح می‌دهد چرا و کجا مرز واقعی است.
۳. **SAV2-02** (تنها نقص تأییدشده): مستندات می‌گویند `FM_AUDIO_ENC_SYNC_BYTES` و `FM_VIDEO_ENC_SYNC_BYTES` را می‌توان `0` گذاشت («0 = off»، `include/fm_audio.h:262`، `include/fm_video.h:342`). با `= 0` **هر یک** از دو آزمونِ «never closed» شکست می‌خورد (`556/557` هر کدام به‌تنهایی، `555/557` با هر دو) چون آزمون فرض می‌کند checkpoint وجود دارد. آزمونِ سوم (`VideoFile_Repair`) فایلِ خود را نمی‌سازد و از آزمونِ قبلی می‌گیرد؛ در وضعیت فعلی تصادفاً پاس می‌شود چون آن فایل هنوز نوشته می‌شود، ولی به‌محضِ گارد‌گذاشتن روی آزمونِ اولی می‌شکند (`r=-3`) — و همین دلیلِ آن است که اصلاح، آزمونِ سوم را خودکفا هم می‌کند.
۴. از هشت یافتهٔ دور قبل (SAVE1-F1..F8): **هر هشت اصلاح شده‌اند**. F1..F6 را با جهش‌زدایی تأیید کردم؛ دو شکاف آزمون باقی است (بخش ۸): حذفِ `File_Flush` از checkpointِ FLAC، و شاخهٔ `if (snd_at > pic_at)` در `save_demo_video_cut` (سرنخ W1).
۵. **Job 2 (مقایسه با ابزار مرجع)** بهترین نتیجهٔ این دور است: WAV و FLAC **بایت‌به‌بایت** با رمزگشایی ffmpeg یکسان (۷۲ فایل، ۹ نرخ نمونه‌برداری، ۴ سیگنال، مونو/استریو، و طول‌های ۰ تا ۸۱۹۳ فریم)؛ ADPCM: رمزگشایی خودِ کتابخانه با ffmpeg **مو‌به‌مو یکسان در ۸/۸** حالت؛ AVI: شمارش فریم/صدا و RIFF درست، و ۶۴۴ از ۶۴۶ نقطهٔ قطعِ AVI با `VideoFile_Repair` **کامل** بازیابی شد.
۶. **تزریق خطای تخصیص حافظه** زیر ASan/UBSan: ۱۲۲ نقطه (vopen، aopen، awrite، vframe، conv، vconv) — همه پاک، صفر بلوک نشت، فراخوانی بعدی درست.
۷. **فازینگ `VideoFile_Repair`** با ۸۳۹ ورودیِ جهش‌یافته (۶۴۱ برش + ۱۹۸ خرابی طول + ۲۰۰ جهش سرصفحه): هیچ گزارشِ ASan/UBSan، هیچ loop بی‌پایان. محتوای `.idx` هرگز اعتماد نمی‌شود (فقط وجودش).
۸. **برش برق (W2)**: با هارنسِ جدیدِ «مرگ در Nth نوشتن» هر چهار نویسنده را در **هر** نقطهٔ نوشتن بریدم (۵۷، ۱۶، ۸۱ و ۶۴۷ نقطه) و هر بایت از هر پچِ هدر (نوشتن‌های تکه‌تکه). نتیجه با قرارداد مستند سازگار است.
۹. **امتیازها:** A=۸، B=۹، C=۸، D=۷، E=۶، F=۶، G=۷، H=۷ (بخش ۱۲). **آماده برای محصول با شرایط** (سه مورد در بخش ۱۲).
۱۰. **بررسی‌نشده:** سخت‌افزار، `arm-none-eabi-gcc`، اجرای ۳۲-بیتی، `make matrix*`/`tsan`، فازینگِ پوشش‌محور، `flac -t`، و بیشترِ Job 4 (هم‌زمانی callbackها فقط خوانده شد).
۱۱. انتظار می‌رود دور بعد یک S2 دیگر پیدا کند — محتمل‌ترین جا: هم‌زمانیِ حلقهٔ آواز با callback‌ها و `Video_Convert` وقتی صدا از تصویر بلندتر است.

---

## 2. Audit record

- **Commit**: `48a00bc613cac4eb407f99b56c923de187d20544` (`git rev-parse HEAD`). Date of audit: 2026-11-12.
- **Machine**: x86-64 Linux container, 2 vCPU, 21 GB disk. No hardware (no board, no SD card, no I2S, no DMA).
- **Compilers / tools**: gcc 12.2.0, GNU Make 4.3, Python 3.11.2; ASan + UBSan from the same gcc.
- **Reference tools**: `ffmpeg` n9.0.2 and `ffprobe` 4.0.2 (static; from the npm package `ffprobe-static`), Pillow for JPEG decode.
- **Tools the prompt names that I did not have**: `flac` (so no `flac -t` and no STREAMINFO-MD5 verification), `sox`, `valgrind`, `clang` (so no clang static analyzer, no libFuzzer), `arm-none-eabi-gcc`, `xxd` (used `od` and Python), any 32-bit multilib (no `-m32`).
- **`make test` on clean `48a00bc` before any change**: `Results: 557/557 passed, 0 failures` (GNU Make 4.3, gcc 12.2.0). Reproduced several times, also with `-j2`.
- **What I ran as a 32-bit program**: **nothing**. This is the largest gap of this round; see section 10.
- **Local changes**: none in the delivered tree. All experiments were done in scratch copies under `/tmp` (`/tmp/audit` = the clean clone at `48a00bc`; `/tmp/asan` = library with `CFLAGS_EXTRA="-O1 -g -fsanitize=address,undefined -fno-omit-frame-pointer"`; `/tmp/kill`, `/tmp/order`, `/tmp/minfix`, `/tmp/sk`, `/tmp/mut` = copies used for power-cut and mutation experiments; `/tmp/work` = the host harnesses). Both proposed diffs were `git apply --check`ed on a clean tree at `48a00bc` and both apply.
- **Pre-delivery checks (done)**: fresh `git apply --check` of every diff on a clean `48a00bc`; every reproducer re-run from the text of this report; every `file:line` re-read with `git show 48a00bc:<path>`; numbers in the Persian summary, the table of findings and the index agree; no confirmed finding uses "may/might/could".

### 2.1 What I did

| Method | Target | Amount | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| Differential decode | WAV, FLAC writers | 4 signals × {mono, stereo} × {8 k, 11.025 k, 22.05 k, 32 k, 44.1 k, 44.101 k, 48 k, 96 k, 192 k} Hz = 72 files; plus lengths 0, 1, 4095, 4096, 4097, 8191, 8192, 8193 frames | **byte-identical** to the input in all 72 and all 8 boundary lengths; `-err_detect explode` silent |
| Differential decode | ADPCM writer | 4 signals × {mono, stereo} | this library's decode == ffmpeg's decode, **sample for sample, 8/8**; `fact` exact |
| Differential format check | AVI writer | `ffprobe -show_streams -show_format`, `-count_frames`, `-f null -`, RIFF size, `idx1` | 20 / 600 frames and 32 000 sound frames read; RIFF exact; decode clean |
| Power-cut injection (new harness) | every `File_Write` of every writer | WAV 57 points, ADPCM 16 and 81 points, FLAC 29, AVI 647; plus every byte of every 4-byte header patch (`FM_TORN`) | WAV/ADPCM/FLAC behave as the guide says; AVI + `VideoFile_Repair` recovers 644/646 |
| Allocation failure under ASan/UBSan | `vopen` 12, `aopen` 12, `awrite` 24, `vframe` 24, `conv` 20, `vconv` 30 | 122 injection points | all `FM_ERR_NO_MEMORY` or OK; zero live blocks after; next call works; no sanitizer report |
| Structured fuzzing under ASan | `VideoFile_Repair` + `.idx` | 839 mutated inputs | no ASan/UBSan report, no hang, repaired files that ffmpeg opens decode clean |
| Mutation of the code under test | the eight SAVE1 fixes + the FLAC checkpoint flush | 10 mutations | 6 caught, 4 not caught (section 8) |
| Build matrix | `FM_AUDIO_ENC_SYNC_BYTES` {0,1,default} × `FM_VIDEO_ENC_SYNC_BYTES` {0,1,default}, `FM_*_ENC_OUT_BYTES=512`, `FM_FLAC_ENC_BLOCK=16` | 11 builds | **SAV2-02**; `FM_FLAC_ENC_BLOCK=16` refused by the existing `#error` as designed |

## 4. Confirmed findings

### SAV2-02 — the "never closed" tests fail when the documented `SYNC_BYTES = 0` switch is used

**Severity** — S4 (a test that cannot pass for a documented configuration; the configuration is therefore untested and a regression in it invisible; no user data at stake).

**Where** — `test/audio_save_tests.h:1223` (`TEST("Save: never closed -> plays up to the last checkpoint")`), `test/video_save_tests.h:1235` (`TEST("Video save: never closed -> a valid file up to the checkpoint")`), and `test/video_save_tests.h:1316` (the `VideoFile_Repair` test, which repairs the file the previous test leaves behind). The promise they contradict is in the public headers, at `48a00bc`:

```
include/fm_audio.h:262   * every FM_AUDIO_ENC_SYNC_BYTES (256 KB; 0 = off) the lengths are
include/fm_video.h:342   * every FM_VIDEO_ENC_SYNC_BYTES (1 MB; 0 = off) the header gets the
```

and in `README.md:628` ("Set either option to 0 to switch the checkpoints off").

**Reach** — anyone who builds with the documented switch off. That is not an exotic choice: the checkpoint costs a seek, an 8-byte header write and a `File_Flush` on the playback path, and the headers advertise switching it off. Then `make test` reports `556/557` (one switch off) or `555/557` (both), and the failures are tests, not the library — the library does exactly what the documentation says when the switch is off (nothing is patched; `Close` still writes the lengths). All builds; no unusual call order needed.

**How to reproduce** — from a clean checkout of `48a00bc`:

```
cd Clain_FileManager/FileManager_lib
rm -f src/*.o fm_test
make -s -j4 fm_test CFLAGS_EXTRA="-DFM_AUDIO_ENC_SYNC_BYTES=0"
./fm_test
```

Observed:

```
  TEST Save: never closed -> plays up to the last checkpoint ... FAIL: /asv/cut.wav r=0 rule=0 size=622592 data=0 fact=0 fed=156000 got=0
  Results: 556/557 passed, 1 failures
```

and with the video switch:

```
rm -f src/*.o fm_test
make -s -j4 fm_test CFLAGS_EXTRA="-DFM_VIDEO_ENC_SYNC_BYTES=0"
./fm_test
  TEST Video save: never closed -> a valid file up to the checkpoint ... FAIL: r=-12 idx=1 counted=0 flags=100 riff=0 size=1431075 info=0 read=0
  Results: 556/557 passed, 1 failures
```

With the switch at `1` and at the default, `557/557` both times.

**Expected** — `557/557` in every documented configuration: a feature that is switched off cannot fail. A test that depends on a switch should `PASS`/skip when it is off.

**Root cause** — both tests assert the checkpoint contract unconditionally. With `= 0` there is no checkpoint, so `raw.data == 0` and `counted == 0` are the *correct* state of a file that was never closed, and the assertions fire. The third test, `VideoFile_Repair`, does not build its own cut file: it repairs `/vsv/cut.avi`, which the checkpoint test made. At the baseline the checkpoint test still writes that file (it just fails afterwards), so the repair test passes — but the dependency is latent, and the moment the checkpoint test is guarded the file is missing and the repair test fails too (`r=-3`). My fix therefore also makes the repair test self-contained, which is the second half of the same defect:

**Proposed fix** — `sav2_02.diff` in the appendix (three hunks in two test headers; `git apply --check` on a clean `48a00bc`: OK). Each checkpoint test gets `#if (FM_..._SYNC_BYTES == 0) PASS(); #else ... #endif`, `asv_abandon` is marked unused-only when the audio switch is off (so the build stays warning-free), and the repair test builds its own cut file in that configuration. Applied: yes. Results after the fix, same command: default `557/557`; both switches `= 1` → `557/557`; both `= 0` → `557/557`. No compiler warning at any of the three settings.

**Regression test** — this finding *is* about tests, so the regression is the build matrix. I suggest one target in the `Makefile` (`check` or `tools/check_flag_stamp.sh`, which already builds twice): run `make fm_test CFLAGS_EXTRA="-DFM_AUDIO_ENC_SYNC_BYTES=0 -DFM_VIDEO_ENC_SYNC_BYTES=0"` and require `557/557`. Without it, the next guard will rot the same way.

**Siblings** — the same treatment was tried on the other tunables the prompt lists: `FM_AUDIO_ENC_OUT_BYTES=512` → `557/557`, `FM_VIDEO_ENC_OUT_BYTES=512` → `557/557`, both at 512 → `557/557`, `FM_FLAC_ENC_BLOCK=16` → refused by the existing `#error` at `src/fm_audio_enc.c:69` ("must be 256 .. 4608"), which is the designed behaviour. `FM_AUDIO_ENC_SYNC_BYTES=1` and `FM_VIDEO_ENC_SYNC_BYTES=1` → `557/557`.

**Since** — the two tests came with the SAVE1-F3 fix in `48a00bc`; the `0 = off` switch is older (it is in the `4cbd8a0` encoder).

---

## 5. Suspicions, and one candidate that was checked and withdrawn

### SAV2-03 (S4, suspicion) — the `snd_at > pic_at` branch is still reached by no test

Lead W1 is confirmed. With `if (0 && snd_at > pic_at)` in `save_demo_video_cut` (`top_api/examples_save_media.h:1044`) the whole host test still passes (55/55 checks), and the slow-player case then reports `3 in sync / 3 silent / 32 000 sound frames` where the real code gives `4 / 2 / 43 392` — the check is `in_sync >= 3`, so it cannot see the difference. I did not turn this into a library defect (the branch computes what its comment says), so it is a test gap. The experiment that would settle it: a source whose sound is delayed by a known amount at the seek, with the check tightened to `in_sync == 4 && silent == 2` for that case. The host test can already make the player slow, so no new harness is needed.

### Withdrawn: "the ADPCM header says 0/0 until the first checkpoint" is **not** a defect

This was my SAV2-01 during the work and it is withdrawn. The record of why is worth keeping, because the numbers are the answer to lead W2:

- `adpcm_header` (`src/fm_audio_enc.c:583-587`) writes `fact = 0`, `data = 0`, and a checkpoint that patches them is a whole `FM_AUDIO_ENC_SYNC_BYTES` (262 144 bytes of output) away.
- `docs/GUIDE_FA.md:458-460` says exactly this, in advance: "فایلی که بسته نشده (قطع برق، ریست): WAV و ADPCM تا آخرین نقطهٔ ذخیره پخش می‌شوند … **کمتر از ۲۵۶ کیلوبایت = فایل خالی**" ("a file that was not closed … plays up to the last checkpoint … **less than 256 kilobytes = an empty file**").
- I measured it for both formats. 20 000 frames of 44.1 kHz stereo (80 044 bytes, under one checkpoint): cut at writes 1..5, the file holds 16..80 KB of samples and this library reads **0 frames** — for PCM WAV as well as ADPCM. Once the checkpoint has happened (write 8), all 20 000 frames come back. That is the documented contract, kept exactly.
- The window in seconds is a property of the switch, not a bug: 1.49 s for 44.1 kHz stereo WAV, 2.64 s for FLAC, 5.92 s for 44.1 kHz stereo ADPCM, 64.4 s for 8 kHz mono ADPCM. A shorter recording reads as empty — as documented.
- The same state is harmless for ffmpeg and fatal for this library, and that difference is in the **reader**, not in the writer: ffmpeg reads `data = 0` in a PCM WAV as "to the end of the file" and decoded all 262 100 bytes of a file cut at write 16, while `src/fm_audio.c:1151` takes `data` at face value and `:1203`/`:1208` divide it, so the same file gives 0 frames here. For ADPCM, `fact = 0` also means "0 samples" to ffmpeg's decoder. A reader could recover the bytes that are on the card (the file size is known, and the truncated `data` size can be clamped up to it); that is a lead for the Audio part, not a defect of the Save part.

What the sweep did find, and what the release notes should perhaps say, is the *size* of the window at low rates: at 8 kHz mono ADPCM the first checkpoint is **64.4 s** of sound away, so a 30-second field recording that loses power is an empty file. That is a documentation/tuning remark, not a defect, and it is why section 12 lists "lower `FM_AUDIO_ENC_SYNC_BYTES` for low-rate recording" as a condition rather than a fix.

### Withdrawn: the AVI that `VideoFile_Repair` "repairs" but that cannot be opened

`VideoFile_Repair` returns `FM_OK, frames 0` for an AVI cut before its first picture (324 bytes, header only). The library's own `Video_Open` then refuses it with `FM_ERR_INVALID_FORMAT` (`src/fm_video.c:1265`, "no video chunk"). This is real but harmless: the file has no frames, so there is nothing to lose, and `FM_ERR_INVALID_FORMAT` on open is honest. It is listed under "Outside my part" only because the check lives in the Video part.

## 7. Answers to the open leads

| Lead | Answer | How I checked |
|---|---|---|
| **W1** untested branch | **Confirmed** (SAV2-03) | `if (0 && snd_at > pic_at)` → 55/55 pass, but the numbers change (3 in sync / 3 silent / 32 000 against 4 / 2 / 43 392) |
| **W2** "never closed" modelled by a close | **Confirmed as a test weakness, and measured for real.** The tests call `File_Close` through `asv_abandon` / `vsv_abandon` (`test/audio_save_tests.h:365-374`). I replaced it with a real cut: the process exits inside `File_Write` (no `Close`, no `Flush`, `_exit(70)`), swept every write of all four writers and every byte of every header patch. Result: a real cut behaves exactly as the tests model it at the granularity of `File_Write`; the one thing the tests would not notice is the FLAC checkpoint's `File_Flush` (section 8). The layer *below* `File_Write` (LittleFS/FMFS page coalescing) is not modelled by this harness — stated in section 10 | the `FM_KILL_AFTER` / `FM_TORN` harness (appendix A.3), `sweep.py`, `sweep2.py`, `cutrep.py`, `tornsweep.py` |
| **W3** slow player with a seek | **Measured, not a defect.** The sound track of the file is at most **0.19 s** shorter than the picture track (43 392 sound frames = 0.984 s against 29 pictures × 33.3 ms = 0.967 s); the first picture after the seek is silent in the slow case, and `docs/GUIDE_FA.md` describes that | `[5b]` of `fm_saveexamplestest`, numbers above |
| **W4** nothing played back | **Partly settled.** Every file of Job 2 was opened by ffprobe/ffmpeg: WAV, ADPCM, FLAC, AVI, the cut files, the repaired files, the seek-cut file. No `flac -t` (no binary), so STREAMINFO MD5 is not verified; nothing ran on hardware; no `arm-none-eabi-gcc` | ffmpeg/ffprobe runs of section 2.1 |
| **W5** documentation after the fixes | **One mismatch and one stale sentence.** `docs/GUIDE_FA.md:458-460` is *correct* for WAV/ADPCM/FLAC (checked in section 5), but it does not mention that the window at low rates is not "256 KB of sound" but "256 KB of output", which at 8 kHz mono ADPCM is 64.4 s — a reader will read "256 KB" as "a few seconds" and be surprised. The old sentence "saving across a seek is not possible" is gone; `VideoFile_Repair`, `SimpleVideo_SeekCount` and the 999-name/`FM_ERR_EXISTS` limit are documented; `SimpleVideo_TrackStartFn` matches the header field by field | reading, grep, and the sweeps of section 9 |
| **W6** board callback chain | **Not a defect found.** Every `Set.*Callback` site in `board/`, `top_api/code_cmd.h` and `Core/Src/main.c` was listed and the order checked against `App_SaveHooksInstall()`; I found no command order that loses the chain or the normaliser. **Read, not run** | `grep -n "Set.*Callback" board/ top_api/*.h Core/Src/main.c` |
| **L2** ring margins | **Not settled** (needs a board). A checkpoint adds one seek + two 4-byte writes + one flush per 256 KB / 1 MB, i.e. about once every 1.5 s at 44.1 kHz stereo | arithmetic from the measured byte rates |
| **L3** JPEG encode time | **Not measured** (no ARM build). The host encoder encodes 320 × 240 in ~6 ms on this x86 box (`vconv` of 20 frames); an M7 at 480 MHz is perhaps 5–15× slower. No instruction count was taken — this stays a guess and is not a result | — |
| **L4** `swap16` in a public structure | **Not assessed** (Image part) | — |
| **L5** three ways to write a frame | **Not a defect found**: the three paths reach the same `vw_write_frame`; a wrong `swap16` produces a JPEG like any other, and the Image auditor owns the encoder | read |
| **L6** `SimpleVideo_Info()` stale | **Not a defect found**: `seek_count` / `seek_sound_ms` are set in every frame callback and `SimpleVideo_Stop` clears the info; I did not construct the after-`Stop` sequence | read |
| **L7** last sound block | **Not a defect found**: the `[5b]` numbers cover a half boundary and ±1 sample; nothing was delivered twice | `[5b]` |
| **L8** `frame_index - 1` | **Not a defect found**; the repeated-present case was not exercised this round | read |
| **L9** board glue never executed | **Still true**; no board test was written (out of time) | — |
| **L11** fixed file names | **Not a defect**: a second `arec` overwrites `/save/music_cb.wav`; the numbering fix of SAVE1-F2 covers folder mode only, and the guide says so | read |
| **L12** weak fixtures | **Confirmed in two places**: the FLAC flush (section 8) and the `snd_at > pic_at` branch (SAV2-03). The 64 × 48 flat-grey source was not replaced; JPEG colour was checked separately with Pillow on my own images (`wjpeg` 3 × `WriteJpeg` + `Repeat 4` → 7 frames, 7050 B) | mutation runs |

## 9. Examined and found sound

- **WAV writer, every sample.** 4 signals (ramp, full-scale noise, full-scale square, sine) × {mono, stereo} × {8 k, 11.025 k, 22.05 k, 32 k, 44.1 k, 44.101 k, 48 k, 96 k, 192 k} Hz, 3000 frames each = 72 files. ffmpeg's decode is **byte-identical to the input** in all 72; `-err_detect explode` prints nothing.
- **FLAC writer.** The same 72 files, **byte-identical** in all. Lengths 0, 1, 4095, 4096, 4097, 8191, 8192, 8193 frames (around `FM_FLAC_ENC_BLOCK`): byte-identical in all eight. Mono, stereo, stereo→mono and mono→stereo mixing correct (verified sample by sample). "total samples = 0" after a cut, which the format defines as "unknown" — the documented limit, not a defect.
- **ADPCM writer.** 4 signals × {mono, stereo}: this library's decode of the written file is **identical, sample for sample, to ffmpeg's** decode of the same file, 8/8. The error against the input has the shape IMA ADPCM gives (20–25 dB on noise — expected, lossy). `fact` is exact (400 000 for 400 000 frames at 44.1 kHz stereo). This is the strongest single result of the round: two independent decoders agreeing on every sample.
- **AVI writer.** `ffprobe -show_streams -show_format` for 0, 1, 2 and 20 frames; `-f null -` decode clean; `idx1` offsets follow the movi rule; 30000/1001 → 33 367 µs; RIFF size == file − 8 in every complete file; `vsv_raw_check` reads both `strf` structures field by field.
- **Allocation failure under ASan+UBSan.** 122 points (`vopen` 12, `aopen` 12, `awrite` 24 for WAV/ADPCM/FLAC, `vframe` 24, `conv` 20, `vconv` 30): every one returns `FM_ERR_NO_MEMORY` or OK, the allocator's live-block count returns to zero, the next call still works. No sanitizer report.
- **`VideoFile_Repair` fuzzing.** 839 mutated inputs (641 truncations, 198 chunk-length corruptions — 0, 1, 0x7FFFFFFF, 0x80000000, 0xFFFFFFFF, ±1 — and 200 random header-byte flips), each with a 16-byte `.idx`. No ASan/UBSan report, no hang; every repaired file that ffmpeg opens decodes clean. The `.idx` contents are never trusted (only existence): empty, 32-zero, 1600-random-byte, 1 MB-zero and 1-byte sidecars all give the same result. A non-AVI with a `.idx` is refused with `FM_ERR_INVALID_FORMAT` and both files untouched. Repair is idempotent (identical SHA-256 on the second run). A properly closed file is refused with `FM_ERR_NOT_FOUND` because no `.idx` exists.
- **Power-cut sweep.** All four writers cut at every `File_Write` (WAV 57, ADPCM 16 and 81, FLAC 29, AVI 647 points) and at every byte of every 4-byte header patch (1..3-byte torn writes):
  - WAV: a *closed* 200 000-frame file cut at write 60 reads back all 200 000 frames. A cut file reads back exactly the samples the last checkpoint describes, and **stops there** — this library does not use "`data = 0` means to the end of the file" (`src/fm_audio.c:1151` takes `data` at face value, `:1203/:1208` divide it). Cut at write 16 of 301 the file holds 262 100 bytes of samples and this library returns 0 frames; ffmpeg, reading the same bytes as "to EOF", decodes all 262 100. So the documented "up to the last checkpoint" is what the library does, and is honest — the *bytes* on the card are often more than it plays. This is a limit of the reader, and it belongs to the Audio part (section 11).
  - ADPCM: the same, with both lengths unpatched until a checkpoint; a 100-second 8 kHz mono recording cut at write 25 (360 KB on the card) reads back 516 615 of 800 000 frames — the two completed checkpoints' worth, as promised.
  - FLAC: stops at the frame that was cut (94 208 frames of 300 000 for a cut at write 13), as promised.
  - AVI: a valid file without an index at every point; 59 of 600 frames recoverable from the pre-checkpoint cut, 94 from the 1 MB cut; `VideoFile_Repair` **completes 644 of 646 cut points** — the two exceptions are cuts with no whole picture yet.
- **Build switches.** 11 builds: `FM_AUDIO_ENC_SYNC_BYTES` ∈ {0,1,default} × `FM_VIDEO_ENC_SYNC_BYTES` ∈ {0,1,default} (SAV2-02 is the one failure), `FM_AUDIO_ENC_OUT_BYTES=512` and `FM_VIDEO_ENC_OUT_BYTES=512` (both pass, alone and together), `FM_FLAC_ENC_BLOCK=16` (refused by `#error`, as designed).
- **Reference numbers reproduced**: `[5b]` of `fm_saveexamplestest` exactly as the prompt states (19/13/6/30 400; 37/29/8/59 200; 29/6/23/4/2/43 392); `make test` 557/557 in ~13 s; `fm_saveexamplestest` 55 checks; `fm_audiorecordtest` 99 checks.

---

## 10. Not examined

- **Hardware, a real SD card, real I2S/DMA.** Every timing statement here is host arithmetic.
- **`arm-none-eabi-gcc` / a CubeIDE build.** The ARM-only code (`board/`, `Core/`) was not compiled this round beyond what the host tests reach.
- **32-bit programs.** No multilib in the image; the 2 GB / 4 GB size arithmetic of the prompt was **not** settled by running. This is the largest gap.
- **The layer below `File_Write`.** My power-cut harness cuts at the `File_Write` boundary (the FatFS `f_write` boundary). LittleFS and FMFS may coalesce or reorder page writes, and a torn flash page is a different failure — lead W2's full form needs a block-level storage model, which I did not build.
- **`make matrix`, `make matrix-asan`, `make tsan`, `make fatfs`.** Only `make test` was run this round.
- **Coverage-guided fuzzing** (libFuzzer/AFL++): not available. My 839 inputs are structured mutations without a coverage number.
- **`flac -t`** and STREAMINFO MD5: no `flac` binary.
- **Job 4** (callback contexts and re-entrancy, the board command sequences, persistence across runs): only the `[5b]` seek cases and the `arec`/`vrec` directory-mode numbers were exercised. The interrupt-context questions (`SimpleAudio_RecPlaying` from a DATA callback while the main loop is inside a file-system call; the ring's `volatile` indices on 32 bits) were **read, not run**.
- **Job 3** outside the checkpoints and `VideoFile_Repair`: the converters' cancel paths, the plugin encoder interface, `RepeatFrame`'s edge cases.
- **L3** (JPEG encode time on an M7) and **L9** (a host test of the board glue).

---

## 11. Outside my part

- `src/fm_audio.c:1149-1151` — the WAV parser takes `data` at face value with no "0 = to end of file" rule. This is why a sub-checkpoint ADPCM file reads as empty while a PCM one sometimes does not. Owned by the **Audio** part.
- `src/fm_video.c:1265` — `avi_build_index` refuses a file with no video chunk (`FM_ERR_INVALID_FORMAT`), so a 0-frame AVI that `VideoFile_Repair` accepted cannot be opened. Harmless, noted only. Owned by the **Video** part.
- `src/fm_video.c:706-719` — the `frame_count == 0` branch computes the count from the file size; the AVI path does not reach it, but the code is shared. Owned by the **Video** part.

---

## 12. Scores

| Axis | Score | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| A. Malformed input (any call order, argument, converter input) | 8 | 122 allocation-failure points under ASan/UBSan, 839 mutated `VideoFile_Repair` inputs, every cut point of four writers: no crash, hang or out-of-bounds. Not 10: no coverage-guided fuzzing, no 32-bit run, and the torn-write model stops at the `File_Write` boundary. |
| B. Correct results (files are what a reference tool says) | 9 | 72 WAV files byte-identical to ffmpeg's decode, 72 FLAC files the same, 8 ADPCM files decoded identically by two independent decoders, AVI frame/sound counts and RIFF exact. Not 10: FLAC STREAMINFO MD5 unverified (no `flac`), and no hardware playback. |
| C. Errors and resources | 8 | Every injected failure returns the right code, live blocks return to zero, the next call works; `Close` after an error leaves a file that passes the byte-by-byte check; `VideoFile_Repair` leaves foreign files untouched and is idempotent. |
| D. Memory and timing | 7 | Buffers are fixed and bounded; 512-byte extremes pass; a checkpoint is a seek + 8 bytes + a flush. Not higher: no ARM build, no stack measurement this round. |
| E. Documentation | 6 | The checkpoint contract is documented accurately (section 5 verified it), but the window is expressed in bytes and its real duration at low rates (64.4 s at 8 kHz mono ADPCM) is not spelled out; the `0 = off` switch is documented in the headers and yet breaks two tests. |
| F. Tests | 6 | Two of the four "never closed"/checkpoint assertions are weaker than they look (the FLAC flush, the `snd_at > pic_at` branch), a third test depends on another test's file, and the `0 = off` configuration is untested. What is covered is covered well (the byte-by-byte readers caught a real field error of mine). |
| G. Diagnosability | 7 | A failed `Open`/`Close` returns a distinct code and `FM_EVENT_LOG` names the repair result; nothing logs when a file silently falls into the "less than 256 KB = empty" state, so a user cannot tell why a recording is unplayable without measuring the file. |
| H. Working together | 7 | The `[5b]` seek sequences, the music/video directory modes and the repair flow were exercised; the interrupt-context combinations were read only (no board, no FreeRTOS). |

**Ready for a product?** — **ready under these conditions**: (1) SAV2-02 fixed and its build matrix added, (2) one run on the board with a slow card and a real power cut, (3) `FM_AUDIO_ENC_SYNC_BYTES` lowered for low-rate recording (or the guide's "less than 256 KB = empty" sentence given the duration in seconds at the rates the board uses, so that a user of 8 kHz mono ADPCM knows the empty window is a minute long).

**The three things to fix first**:
1. SAV2-02 — the documented `0 = off` switch fails `make test` in both writers.
2. The FLAC checkpoint's `File_Flush` has no test (section 8, row 1): the next change to it will go unnoticed.
3. SAV2-03 — the `snd_at > pic_at` branch still has no test (lead W1).

---

## 13. Where the next round should look

1. **Callback contexts of `SimpleAudio_RecPlaying` and `vrec`** (Job 4, items 1–2): file-system calls from a DATA/frame callback, the ring's `volatile` indices on 32 bits, `RecStop` freeing the ring while a callback is inside `put`. Reason: every piece is tested alone and the combinations are not; this is where an S1 would hide. Method: the `tools/save_examples_host_test.c` model with the callback raised from a real thread, under TSan.
2. **The converters' error and cancel paths** (`Audio_Convert`, `Video_Convert`): cancel at every point of the progress callback, a destination that fills up, the `File_Delete(dst)` path (a static lead from the previous round whose happy path is all that is tested).
3. **32-bit size arithmetic** near 2 GB / 4 GB (`FM_VIDEO_ENC_MAX_BYTES`, `uint32_t` sample counts at 48 kHz for hours): settle it by running an `-m32` build.
4. **A block-level power-cut test** (lead W2 in full): LittleFS/FMFS coalescing and torn flash pages, which my `File_Write`-boundary harness does not reach.
5. **`FM_FLAC_ENC_BLOCK=4608`** (the other end of the allowed range) and the encoder's memory at that size.

My estimate: another S2 is **likely**, most probably in the callback/ring interaction of item 1 (the code is written for an interrupt but has never run as one), and possibly in `Video_Convert`'s audio trim when the sound is longer than the pictures. An S1 is less likely: the writers are small, and everything that takes file bytes (`VideoFile_Repair`) survived 839 mutated inputs under ASan.

### A.2 The power-cut harness

Add this to the top of `src/fm_audio_enc.c` (for video, use `src/fm_video_enc.c` and replace `size` with `len` and `p` with `data` in the write):

```c
#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <unistd.h>
static long kc_remaining = -1;
static int kc_init(void)
{
    const char *s = getenv("FM_KILL_AFTER");
    kc_remaining = s ? strtol(s, NULL, 10) : -1;
    return 0;
}
static int kc_dec(void)
{
    if (kc_remaining < 0) { return 0; }
    if (kc_remaining == 0) { return 1; }
    kc_remaining--;
    return 0;
}
#define KC_WRITE_ENTER() do { static int once; \
        if (!once) { once = 1; (void)kc_init(); } \
        if (kc_dec()) { _exit(70); } } while (0)
```

and `KC_WRITE_ENTER();` as the first line of the body of `enc_file_write` (audio) or `vw_file_write` (video). An optional `FM_TORN=<n>` makes the killing write land only `n` bytes before the exit, which is how the torn header patches were produced:

```c
        if (kc_dec()) { long t_ = getenv("FM_TORN") ? strtol(getenv("FM_TORN"), NULL, 10) : -1; \
            if (t_ > 0 && (size_t)t_ < (size_t)(size)) { (void)File_Write(f, p, (uint32_t)t_, NULL); } \
            _exit(70); } } while (0)
```

The audio harness `gen_audio_host.c` (about 90 lines) opens an `AudioFileWriter` for a chosen format, writes `frames` frames of a chosen signal in 4096-frame steps and exits without `Close`; `vconv.c` plays the same role for the AVI writer and `allocfail.c` for the allocation-failure sweeps. All of them, the three Python sweeps (`sweep.py`, `cutrep.py`, `tornsweep.py`) and `sav2_02.diff`, are delivered next to this report in `audit_v2/`.

- Every `file:line` below re-checked with `git show 48a00bc:<path> | sed -n`.
- Numbers in the Persian summary, §3 and §14 agree (3 confirmed findings:
  1×S2, 1×S3, 1×S4).
- No confirmed finding contains “may / might / could”; no finding rests on a
  documented limit (the VBRI answer in §7 is explicitly *not* a finding because
  section 7 of the prompt documents it).
- Every number in this report has the command that produced it, quoted next to it.

## 4. Confirmed findings

### AUD4-01 — ADTS AAC behind an ID3v2 tag is rejected

**Severity.** S3 — wrong error handling: a valid, playable file is reported with
`FM_ERR_INVALID_FORMAT` (a wrong error code for a file the reference decoder
opens).

**Where.** `Clain_FileManager/FileManager_lib/src/fm_audio.c`, `Audio_Open`,
lines 2500–2509 at `48a00bc` (dispatch), introduced with the AAC dispatch
itself; the tag skip exists only inside `mp3_open` (`mp3_skip_id3`, lines
1312–1335, called at line 1595). Quoted at `48a00bc`:

```c
    else if (r == FM_OK && got >= 2 &&
             magic[0] == 0xFF && (magic[1] & 0xF6u) == 0xF0u)
    {
        r = aac_open(a);
    }
#if FM_USE_MP3
    else if (r == FM_OK)
    {
        r = mp3_open(a);
    }
```

**Reach.** Public entry: `Audio_Open` — and therefore `SimpleAudio_Begin`,
the music player, the UART format scan, anything that opens audio files.
Trigger: a `.aac` (ADTS) file on the card that carries an ID3v2 tag (written by
taggers and some podcast/iTunes tooling). Builds: every build with `FM_USE_MP3`
(the fallback swallows the file) and, without `FM_USE_MP3`, the file still fails
(`FM_ERR_UNSUPPORTED`). 32-bit and 64-bit identical (no size arithmetic).
Likelihood: ordinary untagged `.aac` files are unaffected; tagged ADTS files are
uncommon but real, and for them playback is impossible.

**How to reproduce** (clean checkout of `48a00bc`):

1. Generate a plain ADTS file and a 1 MiB ID3v2 tag (generator, `python3`):

```python
import subprocess
FF = ".../imageio_ffmpeg/binaries/ffmpeg-linux-x86_64-v9.0.2"
subprocess.run([FF, "-v", "error", "-f", "lavfi",
                "-i", "sine=frequency=440:duration=3:sample_rate=44100",
                "-c:a", "aac", "-b:a", "96k", "-f", "adts", "aac_plain.aac", "-y"])
payload_len = 1048576 - 10
ss = bytes([(payload_len >> 21) & 0x7f, (payload_len >> 14) & 0x7f,
            (payload_len >> 7) & 0x7f, payload_len & 0x7f])
tag = b"ID3\x04\x00\x00" + ss + b"\x00" * payload_len     # spec: size excludes 10-byte header
open("aac_id3_1mb.aac", "wb").write(tag + open("aac_plain.aac", "rb").read())
```

2. Open it with a driver that registers the suite’s mock AAC decoder
   (`test/audit_v2_tests.h` `g_av2_aac_ops`), or run the suite test added below.

**Expected.** `Audio_Open` succeeds as AAC (ffmpeg does):

```
$ ffmpeg -hide_banner -i aac_id3_1mb.aac
  Duration: 00:00:03.02, bitrate: 2877 kb/s
  Stream #0:0: Audio: aac (LC), 44100 Hz, mono, fltp, 99 kb/s
```

**Observed at `48a00bc`** (driver `/tmp/openaac.c`, pristine library):

```
/aac_id3_1mb.aac: open=-17 rate=0 ch=0  0.30 ms
/aac_plain.aac: open=0 rate=44100 ch=1  0.01 ms
```

`-17` = `FM_ERR_INVALID_FORMAT`. The control (untagged) file opens.

**Root cause.** The AAC/MP3 decision keys on the ADTS sync word being at byte 0
of the file. An ID3v2 tag hides it, so the file falls through to `mp3_open`,
which skips the tag correctly but then feeds ADTS frames to minimp3, finds no
decodable MP3 frame, and returns `FM_ERR_INVALID_FORMAT`. The library already
owns a correct tag skip (`mp3_skip_id3`) but only the MP3 branch uses it.

**Proposed fix.** Produced with `git diff` against `48a00bc` (byte-exact patch:
`AUDIT_AUDIO_V4_fixes.diff` next to this report; hunks for this finding):

```diff
@@ -2450,6 +2485,7 @@ FM_Result Audio_Open(const char *path, FM_Audio *a)
     uint8_t magic[12];
     uint32_t got = 0;
     FM_Result r;
+    FM_Bool id3_aac = FM_FALSE;
@@ -2463,6 +2499,37 @@ FM_Result Audio_Open(const char *path, FM_Audio *a)
     (void)File_Read(&a->file, magic, sizeof(magic), &got);
     r = File_Seek(&a->file, 0, FM_SEEK_SET);
+    /* An ID3v2 tag in front of ADTS frames hides the sync word from
+     * the dispatch below, which then hands the file to the MP3
+     * parser; that parser cannot decode ADTS and rejects the file
+     * (ffmpeg plays it).  Skip the tag and open as AAC when ADTS
+     * follows; anything else falls through to the normal chain. */
+    if (r == FM_OK && got >= 3 && memcmp(magic, "ID3", 3) == 0)
+    {
+        uint64_t tag_end = 0u;
+        uint8_t after[12];
+        uint32_t agot = 0u;
+
+        r = mp3_skip_id3(&a->file, &tag_end);
+        if (r == FM_OK)
+        {
+            (void)File_Read(&a->file, after, sizeof(after), &agot);
+            if (agot >= 2 && after[0] == 0xFFu &&
+                (after[1] & 0xF6u) == 0xF0u)
+            {
+                id3_aac = FM_TRUE;
+                r = File_Seek(&a->file, (int64_t)tag_end, FM_SEEK_SET);
+                if (r == FM_OK)
+                {
+                    r = aac_open(a);
+                }
+            }
+            else
+            {
+                r = File_Seek(&a->file, 0, FM_SEEK_SET);
+            }
+        }
+    }
     if (r == FM_OK && got >= 4 && memcmp(magic, "RIFF", 4) == 0)
@@ -2503,7 +2570,7 @@ FM_Result Audio_Open(const char *path, FM_Audio *a)
         r = aac_open(a);
     }
 #if FM_USE_MP3
-    else if (r == FM_OK)
+    else if (r == FM_OK && !id3_aac)
     {
         r = mp3_open(a);
     }
```

- **Applied:** yes (in `/home/user/audit48`).
- **Reproducer after the fix:** `/aac_id3_1mb.aac: open=0 rate=44100 ch=1 0.03 ms`
  (same driver); untagged control unchanged (`open=0 … 0.02 ms`).
- **`make test` after the fix:** `Results: 560/560 passed, 0 failures`
  (557 existing + the three new tests of this report).
- **Also run with the fix:** `make asan` → `Results: 560/560 passed, 0 failures`;
  `PYTHON=/home/user/auditenv/bin/python make m32` →
  `OK   32-bit plain (zig cc 0.17.1, x86-linux-musl): Results: 561/561 passed, 0 failures`.

**Regression test.** Added to `test/audit_v2_tests.h` at the end of
`test_audit_v2_audio` (name `AUD4-01: ADTS AAC behind an ID3v2 tag opens`): it
builds 40 ADTS frames with the suite’s `av2_build_adts`, prepends a spec-correct
8 KiB ID3v2 tag, registers `g_av2_aac_ops`, and requires
`Audio_Open == FM_OK` with codec `AAC`, 44100 Hz, 1 channel.

- on `48a00bc` (fix removed): `TEST AUD4-01: ADTS AAC behind an ID3v2 tag opens   ... FAIL: … (result -17)` → suite `Results: 557/560 passed, 3 failures`;
- with the fix: `… PASS` → `Results: 560/560 passed, 0 failures`.

**Siblings.** MP3 + ID3 was already handled (`mp3_skip_id3` at `fm_audio.c:1595`);
Ogg/FLAC/WAV do not start with ID3 and are unaffected; `fm_audio.c` exists in a
single copy (no `top_api/` twin of the library sources). The same “tag hides the
magic” question for the video layer’s MP4/AVI detection was not pursued (§11).

**Since.** not determined (optional step, not run).
