### AUD4-02 — mid-file MP3 channel-count / sample-rate change is read as success with wrong labels

**Severity.** S2 — wrong result returned as success: after the change point the
stream keeps delivering `FM_OK` while every sample count, duration and channel
label from `a->info` (latched at open) is wrong; playback through
`Audio_ReadStereo`/`SimpleAudio` garbles the rest of the track.

**Where.** `Clain_FileManager/FileManager_lib/src/fm_audio.c`, `mp3_decode_one`,
lines 1445–1460 at `48a00bc` — the per-frame parameters are adopted without any
check against the open-time values, which `mp3_open` (lines 1619–1640) stores
only into `a->info`:

```c
            m->frame_file_off = m->in_file_off + (uint64_t)m->in_off +
                                (uint64_t)info.frame_offset;
            m->in_off += (size_t)info.frame_bytes;
            m->channels = (uint32_t)info.channels;
            m->hz = (uint32_t)info.hz;
            m->bitrate_kbps = (uint32_t)info.bitrate_kbps;
            m->granule_samples = (uint32_t)samples;
            m->pcm_len = (size_t)samples * (size_t)info.channels;
            m->pcm_pos = 0;
            m->stat_frames++;
            return FM_OK;
```

`m->channels`/`m->hz` change silently; `a->info.channels`/`a->info.sample_rate`
never do, and `mp3_read` (line 1663+, `a->frame_index = m->total_samples / a->info.channels`)
keeps dividing by the old channel count.

**Reach.** Public entry: `Audio_Open` + `Audio_Read` / `Audio_ReadStereo` /
`Audio_ReadMono`, and therefore `SimpleAudio_Begin` playback. Trigger: an MP3
file whose frames disagree in channel count or sample rate — the everyday way
to get one is concatenating a mono and a stereo MP3 (`cat a.mp3 b.mp3 > c.mp3`),
which happens with merged podcasts/radio cuts. Builds: all (no switch involved);
32-bit and 64-bit identical. Likelihood: rare with single-tagged files, common
enough with concatenated ones; ffmpeg refuses/limits the same input (see below),
so nothing else on the stack hides the problem.

**How to reproduce.** Use the two vectors already in the repository:

```sh
cat test/vectors/tone_mono.mp3 test/vectors/tone_stereo.mp3 > vec_mixed.mp3
# driver: Audio_Open, then Audio_Read in a loop until an error, print totals
```

**Expected.** Either the concatenation plays with correct per-part labels
(what a player that reconfigures on change would do) or the library refuses the
change point with an error, as it already refuses >2-channel input (F11).

**Observed at `48a00bc`** (driver `/tmp/readmp3.c`, pristine library):

```
open=0 rate=48000 ch=1 total_frames=3456 codec=MP3
read stop: r=-15 got=0 call=2
total_samples=3456 (frames@ch=3456)
```

`r=-15` is `FM_ERR_EOF` — a clean end of file. The file contains 1152 mono
samples plus 2304 interleaved **stereo** samples; the reader reports all 3456 as
mono (`total / a->info.channels`), so every position, seek and duration after the
change point is wrong, and `Audio_ReadStereo` duplicates each interleaved sample
pair (garbled pitch/tempo on playback). Controls: `tone_mono.mp3` → 1152 samples,
`tone_stereo.mp3` → 2304 samples, both clean. A larger generated pair
(2 s + 2 s, 128 kbps) shows the same class of wrongness:
`total_samples=268416 (frames@ch=268416)` — 88704 mono samples plus 179712
interleaved stereo samples, all counted with the open-time `ch=1`.

**Root cause.** minimp3 reports channels/rate per frame; `mp3_decode_one`
faithfully updates the stream state (`m->pcm_len` uses the *new* count, so bytes
flow), but the public `FM_AudioInfo` is written once in `mp3_open` and never
reconciled, and no error is raised. Consumers (player duration, seek arithmetic,
`Audio_Read*` frame maths, the DMA player’s cached `channels`) all use the
open-time values, so the mismatch is silent.

**Proposed fix.** Treat the open-time parameters as fixed and refuse a frame
that changes them (`FM_ERR_UNSUPPORTED`, the same language the library already
uses for formats it cannot represent). Byte-exact patch: `AUDIT_AUDIO_V4_fixes.diff`;
hunks for this finding:

```diff
@@ -1237,6 +1237,9 @@ typedef struct
     mp3dec_t dec;
     uint32_t channels;
     uint32_t hz;
+    uint32_t open_channels; /* parameters at open; a mid-stream change
+                             * is refused in mp3_decode_one          */
+    uint32_t open_hz;
     uint8_t  in[FM_MP3_INPUT_BUF];
@@ -1442,6 +1445,19 @@ static FM_Result mp3_decode_one(FM_Mp3Stream *m)
                                       m->pcm, &info);
         if (samples > 0)
         {
+            /* The open-time parameters are fixed: Audio_Read consumers
+             * cache info.channels/sample_rate, so a frame that changes
+             * either cannot be represented and is refused instead of
+             * silently mislabeling the rest of the stream. */
+            if (m->open_channels != 0u &&
+                (uint32_t)info.channels != m->open_channels)
+            {
+                return FM_ERR_UNSUPPORTED;
+            }
+            if (m->open_hz != 0u && (uint32_t)info.hz != m->open_hz)
+            {
+                return FM_ERR_UNSUPPORTED;
+            }
             /* Consume the frame exactly as minimp3 reports it: */
@@ -1619,6 +1635,9 @@ static FM_Result mp3_open(FM_Audio *a)
         return (r == FM_ERR_EOF) ? FM_ERR_INVALID_FORMAT : r;
     }
+    /* Latch the stream parameters: later frames must match them. */
+    m->open_channels = m->channels;
+    m->open_hz = m->hz;
     if (m->have_xing)
```

- **Applied:** yes.
- **Reproducer after the fix** (same command):

```
open=0 rate=48000 ch=1 total_frames=3456 codec=MP3
read stop: r=-11 got=0 call=2
total_samples=1152 (frames@ch=1152)
```

`r=-11` = `FM_ERR_UNSUPPORTED`, delivered exactly at the change point; the mono
part is intact; both single-file controls are unchanged (`-15`, full counts).

- **`make test` after the fix:** `Results: 560/560 passed, 0 failures`.
- **Also:** `make asan` 560/560; `make m32` 561/561 (outputs in AUD4-01).

**Regression test.** `AUD4-02: an MP3 whose channel count changes mid-file is
refused` in `test/audit_v2_tests.h`: concatenates the two repository vectors,
opens, reads to termination, and requires the terminating code to be
`FM_ERR_UNSUPPORTED`.

- on `48a00bc` (fix removed): `… FAIL: read r=-15 (result -15)`;
- with the fix: `… PASS`.

**Siblings.** The AAC path parses per-frame `hz`/`channels` and discards them
(`fm_audio.c:2313`, locals at 2302–2303) — same mistake, unprovable on this host
because no real AAC decoder is registered outside the video layer (§5, SUSP-A).
WAV has a single `fmt ` chunk; FLAC/Vorbis/Opus declare channels in the
container header (a mid-file change is a different stream, not representable
either — not tested). The video layer’s audio tracks were not pursued (§11).

**Since.** not determined.

### AUD4-03 — `aac_find_sync` scans one byte per storage call

**Severity.** S4 — wasted time: a resync that must run inside a playback refill
costs 1.3 s CPU on a 2 MiB junk tail on this host — more than the 341 ms
half-buffer deadline at 48 kHz stereo — inside the refill the moment a track
ends in junk.

**Where.** `Clain_FileManager/FileManager_lib/src/fm_audio.c`, `aac_find_sync`,
lines 2144–2171 at `48a00bc`:

```c
static int64_t aac_find_sync(FM_AacStream *s)
{
    uint8_t b[2];
    uint64_t pos;

    for (;;)
    {
        uint32_t got = 0;
        FM_Result r;

        if (File_Tell(s->file, &pos) != FM_OK)
        {
            return -1;
        }
        r = File_Read(s->file, b, 2, &got);
        ...
        if (File_Seek(s->file, (int64_t)pos + 1, FM_SEEK_SET) != FM_OK)
```

Callers: `aac_open` (line 2233) and the resync inside `aac_decode_one`
(line 2324 — reached from `Audio_Read` while a track is playing).

**Reach.** Public entry: `Audio_Read` on an ADTS file whose frames stop before
the end of the file (truncated/concatenated copy with a junk tail), or
`Audio_Open` if the dispatch ever reaches it with the sync not at byte 0 (today
the dispatch pre-filters byte 0, so the *playback resync* is the live path).
All builds; 32/64 identical (no size arithmetic); likelihood: low for ordinary
files, but the cost scales with the junk size and with storage latency (every
byte is one `File_Tell` + one 2-byte `File_Read` + one `File_Seek`).

**How to reproduce.**

```sh
# 40 ADTS frames + 2 MiB of 0x00 (python generator below), then:
#   Audio_Open + Audio_Read to termination, time with clock()
python3 - <<'EOF'
frames = 40; out = bytearray()
for i in range(frames):
    flen = 23; h = bytearray(23)
    h[0]=0xFF; h[1]=0xF1; h[2]=(1<<6)|(4<<2); h[3]=(1<<6)|(flen>>11)
    h[4]=flen>>3; h[5]=((flen<<5)|0x1F)&0xFF; h[6]=0xFC
    for j in range(16): h[7+j]=(i+1)&0xFF
    out += h
open("aac_junk2m.aac","wb").write(bytes(out) + b"\x00"*(2*1024*1024))
EOF
```

**Expected.** The scan reads the tail in a few large reads — milliseconds.

**Observed at `48a00bc`.**

Standalone scan loop (same operations as `aac_find_sync`, posix backend):

```
$ time /tmp/scantest /aac_id3_1mb.aac
sync at 1048576 after 1048576 iters
real	0m0.517s
user	0m0.147s
sys	0m0.369s
```

End-to-end (open + read to termination, 1 MiB junk tail, same mock decoder as
the suite):

```
# pristine 48a00bc
open+read-all: r=-15 samples=0 calls=1  524.0 ms
```

Suite test on `48a00bc` with a 2 MiB tail (see below): `took=1.305 s`.

**Root cause.** The loop advances the file position by one byte per iteration
and pays three FS calls per byte; nothing batches the scan, and the resync runs
inside `Audio_Read`, i.e. inside the DMA refill that has a half-buffer deadline
(341 ms at 48 kHz stereo with `N=32766`).

**Proposed fix.** Scan 512-byte chunks, keeping the last byte for the
cross-chunk pair; leave the file positioned at the sync word exactly as before.
Byte-exact patch: `AUDIT_AUDIO_V4_fixes.diff`; hunk for this finding:

```diff
@@ -2143,36 +2162,52 @@ static void aac_release(FM_AacStream *s)
 static int64_t aac_find_sync(FM_AacStream *s)
 {
-    uint8_t b[2];
+    /* Scan in chunks: the old byte-at-a-time Tell/Read/Seek loop cost
+     * ~0.5 s per MB on the host FS and ran inside the playback refill
+     * whenever a file ended in junk, which is long enough to blow the
+     * half-buffer deadline on real storage. */
+    uint8_t buf[512];
+    uint64_t base;
     uint64_t pos;
+    size_t have = 0;
 
+    if (File_Tell(s->file, &pos) != FM_OK)
+    {
+        return -1;
+    }
+    base = pos;
     for (;;)
     {
+        size_t i;
         uint32_t got = 0;
         FM_Result r;
 
-        if (File_Tell(s->file, &pos) != FM_OK)
+        for (i = 0; i + 1 < have; i++)
         {
-            return -1;
-        }
-        r = File_Read(s->file, b, 2, &got);
-        if (got < 2 || (r != FM_OK && r != FM_ERR_EOF))
-        {
-            return -1;
-        }
-        if (b[0] == 0xFF && (b[1] & 0xF6u) == 0xF0u)
-        {
-            /* rewind so the caller reads from the sync word itself */
-            if (File_Seek(s->file, (int64_t)pos, FM_SEEK_SET) != FM_OK)
+            if (buf[i] == 0xFF && (buf[i + 1] & 0xF6u) == 0xF0u)
             {
-                return -1;
+                /* leave the file positioned at the sync word itself */
+                if (File_Seek(s->file, (int64_t)(base + i), FM_SEEK_SET) != FM_OK)
+                {
+                    return -1;
+                }
+                return (int64_t)(base + i);
             }
-            return (int64_t)pos;
         }
-        if (File_Seek(s->file, (int64_t)pos + 1, FM_SEEK_SET) != FM_OK)
+        /* Keep the last byte: it may pair with the next chunk's first. */
+        if (have > 0)
+        {
+            buf[0] = buf[have - 1];
+            base += (uint64_t)(have - 1);
+            have = 1;
+        }
+        r = File_Read(s->file, buf + have,
+                      (uint32_t)(sizeof(buf) - have), &got);
+        if (got == 0 || (r != FM_OK && r != FM_ERR_EOF))
         {
-            return -1;
+            return -1;           /* end of file, nothing but junk */
         }
+        have += got;
     }
 }
```

- **Applied:** yes.
- **Reproducer after the fix** (same end-to-end driver):
  `open+read-all: r=-15 samples=0 calls=1  2.1 ms` (fresh-clone run: 3.3 ms).
- **`make test` after the fix:** `Results: 560/560 passed, 0 failures`;
  `make asan` 560/560; `make m32` 561/561.

**Regression test.** `AUD4-03: a 2 MiB junk tail after ADTS is scanned in
chunks` in `test/audit_v2_tests.h`: writes 40 ADTS frames, appends 2 MiB of
zeros through `File_OpenEx`/`File_Write`, then `Audio_Open` + read-to-end under
`clock()` with a 0.4 s budget (pristine cost 1.305 s, fixed cost milliseconds —
two orders of magnitude of margin on both sides).

- on `48a00bc` (fix removed): `… FAIL: open=0 took=1.305 s (result 0)`;
- with the fix: `… PASS`.

**Siblings.** The MP3 resync was already fixed in round 1 (F2: batching in
`mp3_fill`); Vorbis/FLAC/Opus use push decoders with no byte-scan of their own.
No other `File_Tell`+`Seek(+1)` loops were found in `fm_audio.c` (grep of
`pos + 1` / `+ 1, FM_SEEK_SET` shows only this function and the MP3 path’s
already-batched refill).

**Since.** not determined.

---

## 5. Suspicions

### SUSP-A — mid-file channel/rate change in ADTS AAC probably mislabels like AUD4-02

What I believe: `aac_decode_one` (`fm_audio.c:2294`) parses the per-frame header
into `hz`/`channels` (locals at 2302–2303, read at 2313) and then uses only
`frame_len`; `a->info.channels`/`sample_rate` are written once in `aac_open`
(lines 2246–2256). A frame that changes the channel count would therefore be
decoded into a buffer whose layout no longer matches the labels — the same
defect class as AUD4-02, one layer over.

Why I could not trigger it: AAC decoding in this library goes through
`Audio_GetDecoder("AAC")`, and the host builds register only the suite’s mock
(`g_av2_aac_ops`), which returns synthetic PCM and never exercises real channel
layouts; no real AAC decoder (FDK/helix) is compiled into the host suite. The
video layer’s AAC path belongs to the video auditor.

The experiment that would settle it: build a host test that registers a real
AAC decoder (or runs the video layer’s), concatenate two ADTS segments of
different channel counts (ffmpeg: `-c:a aac -f adts` twice, `cat`), and compare
`Audio_Read` counts and channel labels against `ffmpeg -i mixed.aac -f wav`.

---

## 6. Verdict on the earlier findings

How to read: “mutation” = I broke the fix the way it was written before (or
inverted its condition) and ran `make test`; a finding is *verified* when the
named test fails under that mutation. Scripts: `AUDIT_AUDIO_V4_mutate_default.py`,
`…_mutate_special.py`, `…_mutate_asan.py` (they print each result; every
mutation was reverted and the tree re-verified clean afterwards).

Summary: 31 rows — 28 fixed (verified by mutation or by an existing exact
test), 3 documented-not-fixed (F20/F27/F29). Of the 28, three fixes have no
test that can fail when the fix is removed (F2’s 2 MiB rejection speed, the
WAV-4 GiB check, F30’s UB path) and one is only defence-in-depth (F11b/`pcmbuf`
rounding).

Also checked per the prompt: the `stb_vorbis.c` local patch is exercised by F1
(mutation M01 crashes without it); `top_api/audio_dma_player.h` is byte-identical
to `examples/audio_dma_player.h` at `48a00bc` (`diff` of the two `git show`
outputs is empty), while `top_api/simple_audio.h` differs from
`examples/simple_audio.h` only in three include paths (11 diff lines) — so every
header-level mutation caught against `examples/` describes the `top_api` copy
too, but note the suite never compiles `top_api/` itself (a mutation placed
only there is invisible: confirmed when M18 first targeted `top_api/` and the
suite still printed 557/557).

## 7. Answers to the open leads

| lead | answer | how I checked |
|---|---|---|
| **W1** stutter regression, who refills when the main loop is busy | **Contract kept — no regression found.** ISR only sets `refill[]` flags (`audio_dma_player.h:1004-1024`); refills run in `AudioDma_Poll` ← `SimpleAudio_Poll` and in `simple_audio_idle`, which `SimpleAudio_Begin` registers with the idle mux (`simple_audio.h:735`); a half that wraps while its refill is still pending raises `underrun` (same lines), the next Poll stops with `FM_ERR_TIMEOUT` (`audio_dma_player.h:1037-1043`) and `SimpleAudio_Poll` auto-restarts the track up to `SIMPLE_AUDIO_MAX_RESTARTS` (`simple_audio.h:1193-1194`). Arithmetic: half period = `N/(rate·ch)` = 1024 ms @16 kHz stereo, 371 ms @44.1 kHz, 341 ms @48 kHz — main-loop blocks of 50 ms and 200 ms are safe at every common rate; a 1000 ms block is not, and behaves exactly as P7 shows. Evidence: harness P6 (`FM_Idle` alone, no `Poll`, still sample-exact, 229362 samples played vs 192000 reference + silence) and P7 (three IRQs without Poll → `underrun` set → `Poll` returns OK after auto-restart, second track-start callback, buffer holds the track head exactly, next refill continues the reference). `docs/REGRESSION_AUDIO_STUTTER.md` was read: the on-record stutter was video/SDRAM bus contention fixed by cache + `FM_Idle` hooks inside LCD/video chunks; the c688b9e playback rework preserved the refill hooks that fix depends on. |
| **W2** sample-exact playback | **Exact — 59/59 checks pass.** `AUDIT_AUDIO_V4_play_exact.c` (attached) drives `SimpleAudio` on the host with the stub HAL, fires the half/full IRQs itself, and compares every DMA half with `Audio_Read` of the same file. P1 one track (STOP): DMA stream = reference then silence, data callback exact, 1 start/1 end. P2 two tracks (16 kHz stereo → 8 kHz mono, NEXT): A exact from sample 0, B after a pure-silence gap of 37362 samples (= A’s EOF tail + draining silence), B exact, post-list silence. P3 seek 0.5 s: DMA = reference from sample 8000, then silence. P4 pause/resume: buffer all zeros while paused, after resume the stream continues the reference exactly, full track played. P5 output CALLBACK: DMA silent, callback stream = exact reference. Output: `RESULT PASS: 59 checks, 0 failures` (`AUDIT_AUDIO_V4_play_exact.out`). The `Audio_Read` side is the round-1 differential (22 files byte-identical to ffmpeg); I did not re-run ffmpeg sample comparison for the playback path (§10). |
| **W3** which output-switch mutations do the tests notice | **2 of 3.** Against `examples/audio_dma_player.h` (the copy `fm_test` compiles, `touch test/main.c` after each edit): (a) delete the CALLBACK-silence `memset` (line 451-454) → `TEST F32: DMA player, output = callback feeds the callback and plays silence ... FAIL: 2 calls, first 0, silent 0` (556/557); (b) fire the DATA callback twice (line 353-359) → **557/557, not noticed** — a real test gap, listed in §8; (c) skip the MONITOR callback (line 438-444) → `TEST F25: … FAIL: pre tap 2048, post tap -1` (556/557). Settings persistence across `Stop`/`Begin` was also verified (P1–P8 all start from the settings left by the previous phase; `sa_cfg`/`sa_volume_pct` live outside the `sa` state that `SimpleAudio_Begin` memsets). |
| **W4** volume above 100 | **Covered.** `test/audit_v2_tests.h:3069` `F33: DMA player, a volume above 100 % boosts and saturates` sets `AudioDma_SetVolume(&x, 2 * 0x7FFF)` and requires `dma[0] == 32767 && dma[1] == -32768 && dma[2] >= 4110 && dma[2] <= 4112` — i.e. full-scale input at 200 % saturates to the int16 rails (no wrap) and a mid-scale sample lands at 2× within the Q15 rounding. The M22 mutation (tap order) also failed F33, showing the assertion is live. |
| **W5** remaining leads of the first report | see the per-lead table below | |

**W5 / Job 3 leads:**

| lead | answer | how I checked |
|---|---|---|
| VBRI headers | **not a defect** — it is inside the documented limit “VBR MP3 without a Xing / Info header: duration and seek positions are estimates” (prompt section 7). Code confirms no VBRI parsing: `mp3_try_xing` (`fm_audio.c:1530-1541`) searches only `Xing`/`Info`; `grep -ri vbri src/ examples/ include/` is empty; non-Xing files fall back to first-frame-bitrate estimates (`mp3_duration_frames`, 1568-1583) and CBR-style seek (1749-1753), which is exactly what the documentation promises. No behaviour worse than written → no finding. (A VBRI encoder is not available here, so nothing was executed against a VBRI file.) |
| channel count changing mid-file | **confirmed defect → AUD4-02** (MP3). AAC variant → SUSP-A. Checked by concatenating the two repository MP3 vectors and a generated pair, reading to the end, comparing counts with the per-file controls. |
| 14-byte `fmt ` chunk | **not a defect — matches the reference.** Crafted both variants; `ffmpeg -v error -i fmt14.wav -f null -` on a physically-14-byte fmt prints `Error opening input: Invalid data found when processing input` — the same rejection our reader gives (`open=-17`, `fm_audio.c:1136` requires `chunk_size >= 16`). The second variant (declared size 14, 16 physical bytes) is a malformed size field; ffmpeg “accepts” it only by misparsing it as 2.00 s of `pcm_u8` instead of 1.00 s of `pcm_s16le` — garbage, not a format we should imitate; ours rejects it. |
| ADPCM allocation derived from `block_align` | **fixed — no defect at `48a00bc`.** `wav_adpcm_serve` allocates `sizeof(*st) + carry_off + cap * sizeof(int16_t)` where `carry_off` rounds odd `block_align` up for alignment and `cap = wav_adpcm_carry_of` is the true sample capacity (`fm_audio.c:377-383`, helpers at 302-337); open-time sanity `block_align >= 4*channels` (IMA) / `>= 7*channels` (MS) at 1186/1195; short final blocks decode only the bytes actually read (comment “bug #3”, 411-436). The suite’s byte-identical-with-ffmpeg ADPCM tests (`test_wav_adpcm_ms`, round 1) still pass; mutation M07 (old duration formula) fails the ADPCM duration test, so the area is live. Edge check for `block_align > 32768` (the one value the open-time minimum checks do not reject): a real `adpcm_ima_wav` file with `wBlockAlign` patched to 40000 — our `wav_adpcm_block_align_of` (`fm_audio.c:302-307`) falls back to 1024 and decodes the file’s true blocks (correct audio), while ffmpeg 9.0.2 frames with the declared 40000 and differs from its own decode of the unpatched file from sample 2041 (the first block boundary) onward: `first audio diff at byte 4082 of 16328`. No real encoder writes such a value and on the only reachable variant of this input our output is the sane one → no finding. |
| tap recorder (`top_api/audio_record_with_tap.h`) | **fixed.** F15 (tap order) and F25 were fixed in `c688b9e`; my mutations M17 (tap order) and M22 both fail their tests (M17 → 2 failures, M22 → F25+F33). `make tapstest` PASS (extracts the `app_audio_hooks.h` block and runs it on the PC). |
| unregistering a decoder while a stream is open | **not a defect.** `Audio_UnregisterDecoder` (`fm_audio.c:2028-2046`) only clears the registry slot under the lock; an already-open stream keeps the `ops` pointer it copied at open, and the contract says decoder tables must have static storage duration (comment at 2049-2051, “every bundled codec does”). New opens after unregister get `NULL` from `Audio_GetDecoder` and fail cleanly (`aac_open` → `FM_ERR_UNSUPPORTED`, 2218-2224). The suite registers/unregisters `AAC` around F17/F18 (`audit_v2_tests.h:2599`, `2737`). No use-after-free is reachable under the documented contract. |
| `aac_find_sync` one byte per storage call | **confirmed defect → AUD4-03** (measured 517 ms/MiB standalone, 524 ms end-to-end, 1.305 s for 2 MiB inside the suite; fixed to milliseconds). |

## 9. Examined and found sound

- **Playback rework `c688b9e`…`48a00bc` (`examples/audio_dma_player.h`, `examples/simple_audio.h`, twins in `top_api/`)** — full read of the fill path and the public API; harness W1/W2/W3 (59 checks, 4 output-switch sequences, pause/resume, seek, track change with rate+channel change, idle-only refill, underrun fault); settings persistence across `Stop`/`Begin`; callback gating matches the header documentation (`simple_audio.h:579-600`, verified line by line against P8’s behaviour: `SPEAKER` → callbacks not invoked, `CALLBACK` → DMA silent, data callback paced by the real sample clock). No sample lost or doubled at any boundary.
- **Job 1 fixes (F1–F30)** — 30 mutations, each with a named oracle; 26 caught, 4 documented as surviving in §8. Thread, opus-lib, and asan variants of the suite all run with their mutations.
- **ADPCM (`wav_adpcm_*`, `src/fm_adpcm.h`)** — allocation, sanity checks, short-read path read line by line (§7 W5); suite byte-identical tests from round 1 still pass; M07 mutation caught.
- **Packet-stream Vorbis decoder (`src/fm_vorbis.c`, Job 2)** — full read of the 546 lines:
  every packet is framed as synthetic Ogg pages (27-byte header, segment table, CRC-32 `vs_crc`
  on the Ogg polynomial, multi-page lacing for packets over 64 KiB, 16 MiB `len` cap at open and
  decode); allocation failures leave the old buffer intact (`vs_page_buf` returns NULL only on a
  failed `FM_Alloc`, state unchanged); unbounded carry is closed by `VS_CARRY_MAX` (128 KiB) with
  `stb_vorbis_flush_pushdata`; `StreamReset` clears carry+pending in every state and is NULL-safe;
  >2-channel headers are refused at open (`info.channels < 1 || > 2` → `FM_ERR_UNSUPPORTED`);
  a mid-stream channel mismatch never produces mislabeled output (pending PCM is published only
  when `channels == s->channels`); `StreamClose` frees decoder/page/state on every path including
  the open-failure paths; float→int16 clamps NaN and [-1,1). Covered by
  `test/audit_v3_tests.h:683` (“Vorbis in MKV: every packet decodes and the track ends”, 45 real
  packets), `:778` unusable headers, `:832-863` argument guards — all inside the 560/560 suite.
  No defect found.
- **FLAC wrapper (`src/fm_flac.c`, Job 2)** — full read: dr_flac compiled `static` (no exported
  `drflac_*` symbols) with allocation callbacks routed to `FM_Alloc`/`FM_Free` (F28-compliant);
  `FM_Flac_Bits` guards (`fLaC` + first block must be STREAMINFO + ≥22 bytes); bit-width refusal
  before open (> `FM_FLAC_MAX_BITS` → `FM_ERR_UNSUPPORTED`); `in_size < 42` → CORRUPTED;
  `out_count == 0` → TOO_LARGE; a container/stream channel mismatch logs and uses the stream’s
  own count with `max_frames = out_count / ch`, so the caller’s buffer cannot be overrun; the
  whole-file open path (`fm_audio.c:513-600`, F14) caps `total_frames` at
  `FM_FLAC_MAX_PCM_BYTES`, starts small and grows ×2, and every early return closes the decoder
  and frees the file buffer. No defect found.
- **MS-ADPCM / IMA edges (`fm_audio.c` + `src/fm_adpcm.h`, Job 2)** — full read of the
  serve/carry/open path: open refuses `block_align < 4*channels` (IMA) / `< 7*channels` (MS) and
  `channels > 2` (`fm_audio.c:1166-1196`); `adpcm_block_frames` returns 0 below the minimum so
  short tails decode to nothing, consistent with `total_frames`; carry capacity (`ba*2` for MS,
  `2*ba-7` for IMA) provably covers the worst block; the final partial block decodes only the
  bytes actually read (bug #3); the `block_align > 32768` fallback was differential-checked
  (§7, W5 ADPCM row). Round-1 byte-identical ffmpeg comparisons and mutation M05 still stand.
  No defect found.
- **Decoder registry** — register/unregister/lookup locking and slot handling read (§7 W5); F17/F18 exercise it under plain and asan builds.
- **`board/app_audio_hooks.h` loudness normaliser** — AGC block read (`:102-160`): peak envelope `×15/16` release fits in `uint32`, gain clamped to `[1.0×, 2.0×]`, apply loop `int32` product fits (32767×512+128), result clamped to int16, division guarded against a zero envelope, snap-down on a loud block before applying (no first-block overshoot after a track change). `make tapstest` PASS.
- **14-byte `fmt `, ADPCM `block_align` allocation, unregister-while-open, tap recorder, VBRI** — each closed with the evidence in §7.
- **Baselines** — 8 build flavours + 3 host tests before any change (§2).

---

## 10. Not examined

- **Fuzzing.** No libFuzzer/AFL/clang on this machine and no new fuzzing was
  written this round. Round 1’s numbers (139 757 WAV / 7 187 MP3 / 86 256 AAC
  / 22 979 FLAC executions, 0 memory errors after its fixes) still stand and I
  did not repeat them; MP3/ADTS with my new harnesses were not fuzzed.
- **Static analysis** (`gcc -fanalyzer`, cppcheck, clang) — tools absent, not run.
- **Stack measurement** (`-fstack-usage`, call-depth sums) — not run; the
  prompt’s request to add call depths to `README.md` “Stack” is unfulfilled.
- **32-bit for my own harnesses** — `play_exact` and the reproducer drivers ran
  64-bit only; the whole suite ran 32-bit (561/561 with fixes).
- **ARM / real hardware** — nothing; the I2S/DMA path is stub-HAL only, as the
  prompt itself records as a known limit.
- **ffprobe** — unavailable; durations/rates read with `ffmpeg -i` instead.
- **ffmpeg sample re-comparison for the playback path** — W2 compares DMA vs
  `Audio_Read`; round 1 already proved `Audio_Read` equal to ffmpeg on 22 files,
  I did not redo that leg.
- **SUSP-A’s experiment** (real AAC decoder + ADTS splice) — not run (§5).
- **Video layer audio tracks, `fm_video.c` audio-in-Matroska/MP4 paths** — only
  noted (§11).
- **`FM_USE_AUDIO=0` stubs** — located (`fm_audio.c:3393-3540`) and read for the
  `Audio_Open`/`RegisterDecoder`/`UnregisterDecoder` stubs; each entry returns
  `FM_ERR_UNSUPPORTED`, but I did not line them up one by one against every real
  signature (partial).

## 12. Scores

| Axis | score | evidence |
|---|---|---|
| A. Malformed input | 6 | No new fuzzing this round; 30 fix-mutations + the whole suite under `make asan` (560/560 with my fixes) produced no memory error, and the three new reproducer files are the only new hostile inputs — a score above 6 would need the fuzzing I did not run. |
| B. Correct results | 7 | Playback is sample-exact against `Audio_Read` in 59/59 checks across 8 scenarios; ffmpeg differential on 5 new inputs found exactly one wrong-success (AUD4-02) and one wrong-rejection (AUD4-01); round-1 differential (22 files) still stands. |
| C. Errors and resources | 6 | AUD4-01 was a wrong error code; unregister/registry, F17/F18 leak oracles and the FMFS write-fault loop all behave; 4 surviving mutations (§8) are error-path/edge checks without oracles. |
| D. Memory and timing | 6 | AUD4-03 (1.3 s inside a refill) found and fixed; allocation sizes in the ADPCM path verified; stack frames/call depths not measured (§10). |
| E. Documentation | 6 | Output-switch documentation (`simple_audio.h:579-600`) verified against behaviour with no mismatch; VBRI/estimate behaviour matches section 7; `README.md` “Stack” still lacks the call depths the prompt asks for. |
| F. Tests | 6 | 33 mutations → 28 caught (85 %); 4 fixes have no failing oracle (F2-speed, WAV-4GiB, F30-UB, data-callback-twice); three new tests added that fail on `48a00bc`. |
| G. Diagnosability | 6 | Auto-restart logs name the error and the count (`simple_audio.h:1193`); AUD4-01 surfaced only as a bare `-17` with no hint of the tag — after the fix the file simply opens; no log line explains a refused mid-file change (the new `FM_ERR_UNSUPPORTED` is silent at the log level). |

**Ready for a product / ready under these conditions / not ready:** ready under
these conditions — host-verified only (no ARM run), with the three AUD4 fixes
applied, and knowing that a data-callback double-fire would go unnoticed by the
current tests.

**Three things to fix first, in order:** 1) AUD4-02 (wrong samples as success),
2) AUD4-01 (valid file rejected), 3) AUD4-03 (refill blown by the byte-scan) —
then close the W3b test gap (a test that counts DATA-callback invocations).

---

## 13. Where the next round should look

1. **Container audio in `fm_video.c` (joint with the video auditor)** — the
   packet-stream Vorbis decoder and the per-call `ops->decode` contract (the
   FLAC/Vorbis wrappers re-open the stream on every call) are read and sound
   inside the audio part, but their callers, buffer sizes and packet framing
   live in `fm_video.c` (`:5925`, `:6611`) and were not audited here; method:
   differential on Matroska/MP4/AVI fixtures + fault injection on the packet
   buffer. Highest remaining yield at the audio/video boundary.
2. **SUSP-A: AAC mid-file parameter change** — build a host test with a real
   AAC decoder registered, splice two ADTS segments of different channel
   counts, compare with ffmpeg; method: differential. Likely same defect class
   as AUD4-02.
3. **Callback reentrancy during refill** — what a DATA callback does when it
   calls `SimpleAudio_Next`, `Stop`, `SetVolume`, `SetDataCallback(NULL)` or
   takes 5 ms (the prompt asks; P8 only covered output switching). Method:
   harness extension of `play_exact.c`; risk: state half-changed under the ISR.
4. **Damaged-stream fuzzing with numbers** — ADTS and MP3 seeds from
   `test/vectors/` under ASan+UBSan, report executions and edge coverage
   (round 1’s runs are a year of fixes old). Method: fuzzing; needs a
   libFuzzer-capable compiler or a hand-rolled mutational fuzzer.
5. **Stack call-depth sums** on the player path (`Audio_Read` → decoder →
   `fill_half` → callbacks) with `-fstack-usage`, to finish the README “Stack”
   table the prompt asks for. Method: stack measurement.

**Estimate of what is left:** another S1 on the playback path is unlikely —
the sample-exact harness and the underrun contract now pin the two places where
S1s lived (buffer boundaries and refill timing). The packet-stream Vorbis
decoder, the FLAC wrapper and the MS-ADPCM edges (Job 2) were read end to end
this round and carry no S-level defect. An S2 remains plausible in SUSP-A
(AAC mid-file parameters), in callback reentrancy (item 3), and in the
video-side container callers (item 1); everything else I expect to come back
as S3/S4 or test gaps.

### A.2 Reproducer generators (all run, outputs in §4)

```python
# AUD4-01: plain ADTS + spec-correct 1 MiB ID3v2 tag
import subprocess
FF = "/home/user/auditenv/lib/python3.11/site-packages/imageio_ffmpeg/binaries/ffmpeg-linux-x86_64-v9.0.2"
subprocess.run([FF, "-v", "error", "-f", "lavfi", "-i",
                "sine=frequency=440:duration=3:sample_rate=44100",
                "-c:a", "aac", "-b:a", "96k", "-f", "adts", "aac_plain.aac", "-y"])
payload_len = 1048576 - 10
ss = bytes([(payload_len >> 21) & 0x7f, (payload_len >> 14) & 0x7f,
            (payload_len >> 7) & 0x7f, payload_len & 0x7f])
tag = b"ID3\x04\x00\x00" + ss + b"\x00" * payload_len
open("aac_id3_1mb.aac", "wb").write(tag + open("aac_plain.aac", "rb").read())

# AUD4-02: concatenated vectors (also done by the suite test)
#   cat test/vectors/tone_mono.mp3 test/vectors/tone_stereo.mp3 > vec_mixed.mp3

# AUD4-03: 40 ADTS frames (same shape as av2_build_adts) + 2 MiB junk
out = bytearray()
for i in range(40):
    flen = 23; h = bytearray(23)
    h[0] = 0xFF; h[1] = 0xF1; h[2] = (1 << 6) | (4 << 2); h[3] = (1 << 6) | (flen >> 11)
    h[4] = flen >> 3; h[5] = ((flen << 5) | 0x1F) & 0xFF; h[6] = 0xFC
    for j in range(16): h[7 + j] = (i + 1) & 0xFF
    out += h
open("aac_junk2m.aac", "wb").write(bytes(out) + b"\x00" * (2 * 1024 * 1024))

# 14-byte fmt differential (both variants)
import struct
rate, ch, bits = 8000, 1, 16
data = b"\x10\x00" * rate
ba = ch * bits // 8; byterate = rate * ba
fmt14 = struct.pack("<HHIHH", 1, ch, rate, byterate, ba)          # physical 14 bytes
payload = b"WAVE" + b"fmt " + struct.pack("<I", len(fmt14)) + fmt14 + b"data" + struct.pack("<I", len(data)) + data
open("fmt14.wav", "wb").write(b"RIFF" + struct.pack("<I", len(payload)) + payload)
```

Drivers (source kept next to this report’s session): `/tmp/openaac.c`
(register mock AAC, time `Audio_Open`), `/tmp/readmp3.c` (open + read loop with
totals), `/tmp/junktail.c` (open + read-to-end with `clock()`), `/tmp/scantest.c`
(the `aac_find_sync` operation sequence standalone). The playback harness is
`AUDIT_AUDIO_V4_play_exact.c` with its output in `AUDIT_AUDIO_V4_play_exact.out`.
